are regarded as local distribution amplitudes with respective to the basis states
\varsigma_{+}
and
\varsigma_{-}
. They are considered to be differentiable functions of coordinates
x^{\mu}
and their freely moving dimension is determined by the maximally correlated motion of spinor field
\psi(x)
. In general,
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
can be either real or complex field operators. In such a representation, the spinor field
\psi(x)
is viewed as a local linear superposition state of two basis states.

It is known that a qubit (quantum bit) as the basic unit of quantum information is in a linear superposition of two states regarded as basis states, which are often represented by Dirac “bra-ket” notation or spin-like “column-row” notation just like any sort of quantum states. The two basis states can conventionally be written as
|0\rangle
and
|1\rangle
(called as “ket 0” and “ket 1”) or
\binom{1}{0}
and
\binom{0}{1}
(called as “spin-up” and “spin-down”). A pure qubit state can be represented as a linear combination of two orthonormal basis states
|0\rangle
and
|1\rangle
or
\binom{1}{0}
and
\binom{0}{1}
,

\displaystyle|\psi\rangle=\alpha|0\rangle+\beta|1\rangle,\quad\mbox{or}\quad\psi=\alpha\binom{1}{0}+\beta\binom{0}{1}=\binom{\alpha}{\beta},

where
\alpha
and
\beta
are constant numbers that represent the probability amplitudes and can in general be complex numbers. The qubit state
|\psi\rangle
or
\psi
as two-component entity is regarded as a global coherent state. When measuring such a qubit in the standard basis, the probability of outcome
|0\rangle
or
\binom{1}{0}
is
|\alpha|^{2}
and the probability of outcome
|1\rangle
or
\binom{0}{1}
is
|\beta|^{2}
. Meanwhile,
\alpha
and
\beta
must be constrained by the normalization condition
|\alpha|^{2}+|\beta|^{2}=1
for the total probability.

Conceptually, a two-component entity spinor field can be regarded as a
local coherent state of qubit
and the field operators
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
are viewed as
evolving local distribution amplitudes
. As two orthonormal basis states span two-dimensional Hilbert space, the local coherent state of qubit forms a vector field with its evolving local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
as two components in two-dimensional Hilbert space.

Let us first consider the simplest case that the evolving local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
represent two independent degrees of freedom, which occurs when
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
are two real fields or a pair of complex conjugated fields. In this case, we can express such a spinor field to be a
local coherent state of qubit
in a linear superposition of two orthonormal basis states with two local distribution amplitudes,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm}\psi_{s}(x)\varsigma_{s}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)}\equiv\binom{\psi_{0}(x)}{\psi_{1}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm}\psi_{s}(x)\zeta_{s}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{0}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{0}}^{\ast}(x)}\equiv\sum_{s=\pm}\tilde{\psi}_{s}(x)\varsigma_{s}=\binom{\tilde{\psi}_{+}(x)}{\tilde{\psi}_{-}(x)},

(36)

where the superscript “1” in
\mathsf{Q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
labels the qubit number
Q_{N}=1
. We have used the following definitions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x):\quad\psi_{+}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)\equiv\psi_{0}(x)=\psi_{0}^{\ast}(x),\quad\psi_{-}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}\equiv\psi_{1}(x)=\psi_{1}^{\ast}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x):\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{0}}(x)\equiv\tilde{\psi}_{+}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)+i\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x))=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{0}(x)+i\psi_{1}(x)),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{0}}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\tilde{\psi}_{-}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)-i\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x))=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{0}(x)-i\psi_{1}(x)),

where two real local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)
are regarded as two
real local states of zeroqubit
as hinted in the superscript “0” in
\mathsf{Q}^{0}
with zero qubit number
Q_{N}=0
. We have introduced alternatively in Eq.(
III.1
) an equivalent orthonormal basis states
\zeta_{s}
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{+}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{1}{1},\quad\zeta_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{i}{-i},

(37)

where
\zeta_{s}
(
s=\pm
) form an orthonormal
complex-conjugated basic qubit-basis

\{\zeta_{s}\}
, which spans a complex-conjugated two-dimensional Hilbert space.

The two kinds of local coherent states of qubit,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
, satisfy the following complex-conjugated conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)=\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x),\quad\sigma_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},

(38)

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
is considered as a
real local coherent state of qubit
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
as a
self-conjugated local coherent state of qubit
indicated from the subscript “
\mathsf{S}
” in
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
.

When two local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{+}(x)
and
\psi_{-}(x)
are all complex fields,
\psi(x)
is regarded as a
complex local coherent state of qubit
,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s=\pm}\psi_{s}(x)\varsigma_{s}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}(x)},

(39)

where the subscript “
\mathsf{C}
” in
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}
reflects the complex feature for local coherent state
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and zeroqubit local states
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}(x)
. Note that
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
as a complex local coherent state of qubit involves in general four real independent degrees of freedom.

The two basic qubit-bases enable us to define conceptually the real two-component entity spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and self-conjugated two-component entity spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
as well as complex two-component entity spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
to be as local coherent states of qubit. We would like to address that, unlike the ordinary global coherent state of qubit in which the global amplitudes are explained as the probability amplitudes of basis states for measuring the qubits in a standard basis based on the Born’s rule of quantum mechanics, while the evolving local distribution amplitudes of spinor field are considered as
dynamic field operators
.

To reflect above conceptual definitions on two-component spinor field and also for compact terminology, we may refer to
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
as
real uniqubit-spinor field
,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
as
self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
as
complex uniqubit-spinor field
. In the following discussions,
uniqubit-spinor field
will mean
real uniqubit-spinor field
unless otherwise specified.

In order to investigate the kinematical evolution of uniqubit-spinor field, we are going to apply the maximum coherence motion principle as guiding principle to build the action of uniqubit-spinor field. Let us first consider the action of freely moving uniqubit-spinor field, which has the following general form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int[dx]\,\{\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)-\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi_{p}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)\},

(40)

with
\lambda_{1}
the real coupling constant and
\phi_{p}(x)
real scalar fields. Following along the maximum coherence motion principle as guiding principle, we are able to figure out explicitly all real symmetric normalized basis
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices from all
2\times 2
matrices as follows:

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix};\quad\Upsilon^{1}=\sigma_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix};\quad\Upsilon^{2}=\sigma_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},

(41)

which satisfy Clifford algebra relations given in Eq.(
II.3
) with
a=0,1,2
. Obviously, there exists only a single pure imaginary antisymmetric normalized basis
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}=\sigma_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-i\\
i&0\end{pmatrix},

(42)

which meets to Clifford algebra relations in Eqs.(
23
) and (
24
) with
q_{c}=1
. Where
\sigma_{i}
(
i=1,2,3
) are Pauli matrices.

From the action of uniqubit-spinor field given in Eq.(
40
), the maximally correlated motion is characterized by three real symmetric normalized basis
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices, and the motion-irrelevance scalar coupling is described by the single
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix, which brings
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{c}}=D_{h}=3,\quad\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=1.

(43)

Where the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge determines three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime with one temporal dimension and two spatial dimensions. The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge brings on the scalar coupling with one real scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
in associating with the single
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix. It is noticed that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
is directly associated to the qubit number
Q_{N}
.

III.2
Properties of uniqubit-spinor field and three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime under intrinsic discrete symmetries

To study the intrinsic properties of uniqubit-spinor field and scalar field as well as Minkowski spacetime based on the action in Eq.(
40
), we should firstly discuss basic discrete symmetries under the operations: charge-conjugation (
\mathcal{C}
), parity-inversion (
\mathcal{P}
) and time-reversal (
\mathcal{T}
).

Let us begin with rewritting the action in Eq.(
40
) into the conventional one by ultilizing the so-called
\gamma
-matrices. Both uniqubit-spinor field and self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field may be referred to as
Taijion
or
Taijion spinor field
denoted as
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)
(
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}=\mathsf{Q}^{1},\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
), which has the conventional action as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{3}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\tilde{\gamma}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\},

(44)

where we have used the following definitions for
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)
and
\gamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)=\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}\gamma^{0},\quad\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2},\quad\gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{1},\quad\gamma^{2}=-i\sigma_{3},\quad\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)=\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}\gamma^{0},\quad\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3},\quad\gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{2},\quad\gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{1},\quad\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma_{0},

(45)

with
\gamma^{a}=\tilde{\Upsilon}\Upsilon^{a}
(
a=0,1,2
) for
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
, and
\gamma^{0}=\tilde{\Upsilon}\Upsilon^{2},\gamma^{1}=\tilde{\Upsilon}\Upsilon^{0},\gamma^{2}=\tilde{\Upsilon}\Upsilon^{1}
for
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
. It is noticed that in the real spinor presentation
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)
, all
\gamma
-matrices are imaginary ones. The above defined
\gamma
-matrices satisfy the following anti-commuting relations:

\displaystyle\{\gamma^{a},\gamma^{b}\}=2\eta^{ab},\quad\eta^{ab}=\mathop{\rm diag}(1,-1,-1).

(46)

Two actions for the uniqubit-spinor field and self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field can be related via the following unitary transformation between two qubit-spinor fields:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)=U\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x),\quad U^{\dagger}U=1,\quad U=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1&i\\
1&-i\end{pmatrix}.

It is easy to verify that
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
satisfy the following self-conjugate conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{c}(x)=C_{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{T}(x)=C_{2}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{2}\equiv C_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}=\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}^{c}(x)=C_{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}^{T}(x)=C_{2}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{2}\equiv C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}=i\gamma^{0}\gamma^{2}=-i\sigma_{2},

where
\psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
define the charge-conjugated ones of
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
.

Let us now demonstrate how the action in Eq.(
44
) preserves the invariance under discrete symmetries: charge-conjugation (
\mathcal{C}
), parity-inversion (
\mathcal{P}
), time-reversal (
\mathcal{T}
) and
W
-parity operation (
\mathcal{W}
). Explicitly, the Taijion spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)
and scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
have the following transformation properties:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{c}(x)=C_{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{T}(x),

\displaystyle C_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}C_{2}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\quad C_{2}^{T}=-C_{2},\quad C_{2}^{\dagger}=C_{2}^{-1},

\displaystyle C_{2}=\begin{cases}\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
i\gamma^{0}\gamma^{2}=-i\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\phi_{1}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}=\phi_{1}(x),

for the charge conjugation, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{p}(x)=P_{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x^{0},-x^{k}),

\displaystyle P_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}P_{2}=\gamma^{a\,\dagger},\quad a=0,1,2,

\displaystyle P_{2}=\begin{cases}-i\gamma^{0}=-i\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\phi_{1}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}=\phi_{1}(x^{0},-x^{k}),\quad k=1,2,

for the parity-inversion, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{t}(x)=T_{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(-x^{0},x^{1},-x^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}T_{2}=\gamma^{a\,T},\;(a=0,1),\quad T_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{2}T_{2}=-\gamma^{2\,T},

\displaystyle T_{2}=\begin{cases}\gamma^{0}\gamma^{2}=\sigma_{1},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
i\gamma^{1}\gamma^{2}=-\sigma_{3},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\phi_{1}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}=\phi_{1}(-x^{0},x^{1},-x^{2}),

for the time-reversal with the normal definition.

It is noticed that the spatial coordinate
x^{2}
gets an unusual transformation property under the time-reversal operation as it undergoes a flip in sign so as to ensure the invariance of the action. That means there is an unusual
\mathcal{CPT}
operation for the Taijion spinor field in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. To obtain a usual transformation property similar to the ordinary
\mathcal{CPT}
operator acting on a massive Dirac fermion in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, let us introduce the so-called W-parity operation
GGFT6D
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{w}(x)=W_{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x^{\mu},-x^{2}),\quad\mu=0,1,

\displaystyle W_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}W_{2}=-\gamma^{a},\;(a=0,1),\quad W_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{2}W_{2}=\gamma^{2},

\displaystyle W_{2}=\begin{cases}i\gamma^{2}=\sigma_{3},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
i\gamma^{2}=-\sigma_{1},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\phi_{1}(x)\mathcal{W}^{-1}=-\phi_{1}(x^{\mu},-x^{2}),\quad\mu=0,1,

which is viewed as a kind of chirality reflection.

Such a W-parity operation motivates us to define the combined operator
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}=\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}
as a generalized time-reversal operator, it can bring on the following proper transformation:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{\tilde{t}}(x)=\tilde{T}_{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(-x^{0},x^{k}),

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}\hat{T}_{2}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\;\;a=0,1,2,

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{2}=\begin{cases}i\gamma^{0}=i\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
-\gamma^{1}=-i\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\phi_{1}(x)\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}=-\phi_{1}(-x^{0},x^{k}),\quad k=1,2,,

which recovers the usual time-reversal operation.

It is clear that we should adopt the combined joint operator
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPTW}=\mathcal{CP\tilde{T}}
for the Taijion spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)
in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, so that we are able to arrive at the following conventional transformation property:

\displaystyle\varTheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{T}(-x),\quad\Theta^{-1}\gamma^{a}\Theta=\gamma^{a\,\dagger},

\displaystyle\Theta=WTPC=\begin{cases}\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}},\\
\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\varTheta\phi_{1}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=-\phi_{1}(-x),

which indicates that the combined joint operator
\varTheta
, in comparison to the ordinary
\mathcal{CPT}
operator for a massive Dirac fermion in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, becomes an essential one for the Taijion spinor field in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

III.3
Presence of spatial dimensions in correspondence to rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin group symmetry

The maximally correlated motion of uniqubit-spinor field as local coherent state of qubit determines the dimension of spacetime. Let us explicitly illustrate how the rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin group symmetry of uniqubit-spinor field correlates to spatial dimensions of Minkowski spacetime.

In general, we are not able to distinguish two local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{0}(x)
and
\psi_{1}(x)
of uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
, the action should be invariant under a rotational transformation between two amplitudes
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
. Explicitly, such a rotational transformation can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\binom{\psi_{0}}{\psi_{1}}\to\binom{\psi^{\prime}_{0}}{\psi^{\prime}_{1}}\equiv e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\binom{\psi_{0}}{\psi_{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\frac{\varpi}{2}&\sin\frac{\varpi}{2}\\
-\sin\frac{\varpi}{2}&\cos\frac{\varpi}{2}\end{pmatrix}\binom{\psi_{0}}{\psi_{1}},

with
\varpi
a constant parameter.
\Sigma
is the group generator of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(2), which is given by the commutator of
\gamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\Sigma=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{2}=\frac{i}{4}[\gamma^{1},\gamma^{2}],\quad e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\in SP(2).

Under such a SP(2) group transformation, the
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
behave as a vector in the spinor representation and have the following transformation property:

\displaystyle e^{-i\varpi\Sigma}\gamma^{a}e^{i\varpi\Sigma}=L^{a}_{\;b}\gamma^{b},\;\;a,b=1,2,

\displaystyle L^{a}_{\;b}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\varpi&\sin\varpi\\
-\sin\varpi&\cos\varpi\end{pmatrix}\in SO(2),

where the matrix
L^{a}_{\;b}
belongs to the element of orthogonal rotation group SO(2).

To ensure the invariance of the action under transformation of rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(2), it must bring the spatial Cartesian coordinates
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=1,2
) to have a coincidental transformation under orthogonal rotation group SO(2),

\displaystyle x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}\,x^{\nu},\quad L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\varpi&\sin\varpi\\
-\sin\varpi&\cos\varpi\end{pmatrix}\in SO(2).

So that the action becomes invariant only under simultaneous SP(2) and SO(2) transformations for uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and spatial coordinates
x^{\mu}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x),\quad x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu},

\displaystyle e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\in SP(2),\quad L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}=L^{a}_{\;b}\in SO(2),\quad(a,b,\mu,\nu=1,2).

(47)

In terms of the self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
, such a rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin transformation is equivalent to an U(1) spin charge transformation:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)=e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)=\binom{e^{-\frac{1}{2}i\varpi}\psi(x)}{e^{\frac{1}{2}i\varpi}\psi^{\ast}(x)},

\displaystyle\Sigma=-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{3}=\frac{i}{4}[\gamma^{1},\gamma^{2}],\quad e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\in U(1).

(48)

Therefore, the presence of spatial dimensions is in correspondence to the rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry. This is because the invariance of the action under the transformation of rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(2) (or U(1) ) for freely moving uniqubit-spinor field naturally brings on the transformation of orthogonal rotation group SO(2) for Cartesian coordinates
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=1,2
) so as to match coincidentally to the transformation of rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group. Mathematically, it is indicated from the isomorphic property of symmetry groups,

\displaystyle SP(2)\cong U(1)\cong SO(2),

(49)

where the rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin group SP(2) in two-dimensional Hilbert space doubly covers the orthogonal rotation group SO(2) in Euclidean space of coordinates. This is because each rotation can be obtained in two inequivalent ways as the endpoint of a path.

III.4
Presence of temporal dimension in correspondence to non-homogeneous scaling symmetry

The action in Eq.(
44
) is naturally invariant under homogeneous scaling transformation as shown in Eq.(
II.6
). It is interesting to consider a non-homogeneous scaling transformation between two local distribution amplitudes of uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}
,

\displaystyle\binom{\psi_{0}}{\psi_{1}}\to\binom{\psi^{\prime}_{0}}{\psi^{\prime}_{1}}\equiv e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\binom{\psi_{0}}{\psi_{1}}=\binom{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varpi}\psi_{0}}{e^{\frac{1}{2}\varpi}\psi_{1}},

with
\varpi
a constant parameter.
\Sigma
is viewed as the group generator of
U^{\ast}(1)
for non-homogeneous scaling transformation, which can be given by the commutator of
\gamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\Sigma=\frac{i}{4}[\gamma^{0},\gamma^{1}]=\frac{1}{2}i\sigma_{3},\qquad e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\in U^{\ast}(1).

Such a non-homogeneous scaling transformation is regarded as a boost
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin transformation. The
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1
) transform as a vector in Hilbert space with the following transformation property:

\displaystyle e^{-i\varpi\Sigma}\gamma^{a}e^{i\varpi\Sigma}=L^{a}_{\;b}\gamma^{b},\;\;a,b=0,1,

\displaystyle L^{a}_{\;b}=\begin{pmatrix}\cosh\varpi&-\sinh\varpi\\
-\sinh\varpi&\cosh\varpi\end{pmatrix}\in SO(1,1),

where the matrix
L^{a}_{\;b}
belongs to the element of Lorentz-type group SO(1,1).

To obtain the invariant action under non-homogeneous scaling transformation, it requires that both temporal and spatial coordinates
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=0,1
) must simultaneously transform as follows:

\displaystyle x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu},\quad\mu,\nu=0,1,

\displaystyle L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}=L^{a}_{\;b}=\begin{pmatrix}\cosh\varpi&-\sinh\varpi\\
-\sinh\varpi&\cosh\varpi\end{pmatrix}\in SO(1,1).

Namely, the action presented in Eq.(
44
) gets invariance only when the uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
together with the temporal and spatial coordinates
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=0,1
) transform explicitly as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{-i\varpi\Sigma}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)=\binom{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varpi}\psi_{0}(x)}{e^{\frac{1}{2}\varpi}\psi_{1}(x)},

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}\,x^{\nu}\equiv\delta^{\mu}_{\;a}\delta_{\nu}^{\;b}L^{a}_{\;b}\,x^{\nu},\quad(\mu,\nu=0,1).

(50)

There is a similar feature for the self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
, the action in Eq.(
44
) becomes invariant only when
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
and
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=0,1
) transform simultaneously as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x^{\prime})=e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}\cosh\frac{\varpi}{2}&-\sinh\frac{\varpi}{2}\\
-\sinh\frac{\varpi}{2}&\cosh\frac{\varpi}{2}\end{pmatrix}\binom{\psi(x)}{\psi^{\ast}(x)},

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu}\equiv\delta^{\mu}_{\;a}\delta_{\nu}^{\;b}L^{a}_{\;b}x^{\nu},\quad L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}\in SO(1,1),\quad(\mu,\nu=0,1),

\displaystyle\Sigma=\frac{i}{4}[\gamma^{0},\gamma^{1}]=i\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{1},\quad e^{i\varpi\Sigma}\in SP(1,1),

(51)

where
\Sigma
is the group generator of boost
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(1,1).

The invariance of the action under the transformation of non-homogeneous scaling group
U^{\ast}(1)
(or boost
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin group SP(1,1) ) for uniqubit-spinor field demands simultaneously the coincidental transformation of Lorentz-type group SO(1,1) for temporal and spatial coordinates, which can be characterized from the isomorphic property of symmetry groups,

\displaystyle U^{\ast}(1)\cong SP(1,1)\cong SO(1,1).

(52)

The variables
x^{\mu}
(
\mu=0,1
) and
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1
) transform as two vectors of symmetry group SO(1,1)
\cong
SP(1,1) with respective to both coordinate and non-coordinate two-dimensional spacetimes. In general, we can define SO(1,1) invariant scalar product of coordinates
x^{\mu}
and
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
as follows:

\displaystyle x^{\mu}x_{\mu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}x^{\mu}x^{\nu}=(x^{0})^{2}-(x^{1})^{2},

\displaystyle\gamma^{a}\gamma_{a}=\eta_{ab}\gamma^{a}\gamma^{b}=(\gamma^{0})^{2}-(\gamma^{1})^{2},

where two components
x^{0}
(
\gamma^{0}
) and
x^{1}
(
\gamma^{0}
) exhibit a different feature as indicated with a flip in sign. When the coordinate
x^{1}
is regarded as a Cartesian coordinate of space, the coordinate
x^{0}
is designated as a temporal coordinate.

Therefore, the presence of the temporal dimension is in correspondence to the non-homogeneous scaling symmetry, which is attributed to the requirement on the invariance of the action under non-homogeneous scaling transformation (or boost
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin transformation) for uniqubit-spinor field. In another word, the existence of one temporal dimension implies that the local distribution amplitudes of uniqubit-spinor field are varying as the evolution of temporal coordinate.

III.5
Maximal coherent symmetry of uniqubit-spinor field and the equation of motion with dynamic
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime

The existence of basic scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
and its coupling to uniqubit-spinor field bring on a global scaling symmetry for the action presented in Eq.(
44
),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{\varpi}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x),\quad\phi_{1}(x)\to\phi_{1}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{\varpi}\phi_{1}(x),

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}=e^{-\varpi}x^{\mu},\quad e^{-\varpi}\in SC(1),\;\;e^{\varpi}\in SG(1),

(53)

with
\varpi
a constant parameter.

Let us consider the scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
to be a dynamical field. A global scaling invariant action in Eq.(
44
) for uniqubit-spinor field with dynamic scalar field is generalized to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{3}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\tilde{\phi}^{2}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)

(54)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{\phi}(x)\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}(x)-\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{Q}\tilde{\phi}^{6}(x)\},

with
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}=(\mathsf{Q}^{1},\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1})
and
\lambda_{Q}
a real coupling constant for scalar interaction. Where we have made a redefinition,

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(x)\equiv\tilde{\phi}^{2}(x),

which is referred to as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
for convenience of mention.

When making simultaneously the rotational
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and non-homogeneous scaling (or boost
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin) as well as global scaling transformations together with translational and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin transformations, we arrive at an associated symmetry for the action presented in Eq.(
44
) in three-dimensional spacetime. Explicitly, the action becomes invariant under the following transformations for uniqubit-spinor field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field together with coordinates:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=S(L)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x);\quad\tilde{\phi}(x)\to\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\tilde{\phi}(x),

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu},\quad S^{-1}(L)\gamma^{a}S(L)=L^{a}_{\;b}\gamma^{b},

\displaystyle S(L)=e^{i\Sigma^{ab}\varpi_{ab}}\in SP(1,2),\quad L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}=L^{a}_{\;b}\in SO(1,2),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}^{\prime}(x)=S_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x);\quad\tilde{\phi}(x)\to\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}(x)=S_{\pm}\tilde{\phi}(x),

\displaystyle S_{\pm}\in SP(1)\cong O(1),

(55)

with
a,b,\mu,\nu=0,1,2
. Where
S(L)
and
L^{a}_{\;b}
are the group elements given explicitly by the following matrices:

\displaystyle S(L)=e^{i\Sigma^{ab}\varpi_{ab}}=\cos\frac{\varpi}{2}+i\gamma^{a}n_{a}\sin\frac{\varpi}{2},

\displaystyle L^{a}_{\;b}=(\eta^{a}_{\;b}-n^{a}n_{b})\cos\varpi+n^{a}n_{b}+\epsilon^{a}_{\;\;bc}n^{c}\sin\varpi,

(56)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\varpi=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\varpi_{ab}\varpi^{ab}}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varpi_{12}^{2}-\varpi_{01}^{2}-\varpi_{02}^{2}},

\displaystyle n^{a}=\frac{1}{4\varpi}\epsilon^{abc}\varpi_{bc},\quad\epsilon^{012}=1,\quad n_{a}n^{a}=1.

Therefore, the action in Eq.(
54
) possesses
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,2) and global scaling symmetry SG(1) together with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1) in Hilbert space, which is simultaneously associated with Poincaré-type (inhomogeneous Lorentz-type) group symmetry PO(1,2) and conformal scaling symmetry SC(1) in Minkowski spacetime. Such a general symmetry can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,2}\ltimes SO(1,2)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,2)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(1)

(57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,2)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,2)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(1),

where Poincaré-type group symmetry is a semidirect product group symmetry of Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,2) and translational group symmetry P
1,2
,

\displaystyle PO(1,2)=P^{1,2}\ltimes SO(1,2).

(58)

The
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(1,2) in Hilbert space of uniqubit-spinor field is isomorphic to the orthogonal Lorentz-type symmetry group SO(1,2) in Minkowski spacetime of coordinates, and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1) for uniqubit-spinor field is isomorphic to the group O(1) for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field. Note that we have adopted the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
” to indicate an
associated symmetry
in which the transformations of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,2) and global scaling symmetry SG(1) in Hilbert space must be coincidental to those of Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,2) and scaling symmetry SC(1) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime.

As all
\gamma
-matrices are imaginary in the real representation of uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
, the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(1,2) is isomorphic to special linear symmetry group SL(2,R). For the self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
, its
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group is isomorphic to special symmetry group
SU(1,1)
. In general, we can present the symmetry property of the action in Eq.(
54
) as follows:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\begin{cases}SP(1,2)\cong SL(2,R)\cong SO(1,2),&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)\\
SP(1,2)\cong SU(1,1)\cong SO(1,2),&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x).\end{cases}

(59)

From the principle of least action, we obtain the following equations of motion for the uniqubit-spinor field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field:

\displaystyle\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)-\lambda_{1}\tilde{\phi}^{2}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)=0,

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}(x)+\lambda_{Q}\tilde{\phi}^{5}(x)+\lambda_{1}\tilde{\phi}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)=0,

(60)

which are globally scaling invariant.

IV
Local coherent-qubits motion postulate and the nature of biqubit-spinor field and triqubit-spinor field as Majorana and Dirac fermions with appearance of 4D and 6D Minkowski spacetimes

Following along the maximum coherence motion principle as guiding principle, we have demonstrated that the local coherent state of qubit with real distribution amplitudes of two orthonormal qubit-basis states presents a uniqubit-spinor field with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{c}}
=3 and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}
=1. Such a Taijion spinor field spans two-dimensional Hilbert space with maximally correlated motion in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. For a generalized consideration, we are led to make a
local coherent-qubits motion postulate
upon which the qubit-spinor field is supposed to be the basic constituent of matter. As an explicit illustration, we will demonstrate in this section that neutrinos and charged leptons/quarks as basic constituents of matter are viewed as local coherent states of 2-qubit (
Q_{N}
=2) and 3-qubit (
Q_{N}=
3) with respective to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=2
and
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=3
. It will be shown that neutrinos as Majorana fermions and charged leptons/quarks as Dirac fermions can be characterized correspondingly by
biqubit-spinor field
and
triqubit-spinor field
or equivalently by
complex uniqubit-spinor field
and
complex biqubit-spinor field
. They span four-dimensional and eight-dimensional real Hilbert spaces or two-dimensional and four-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces, respectively. We will prove that the maximally correlated motions of biqubit-spinor field and triqubit-spinor field bring on four-dimensional and six-dimensional Minkowski spacetimes, respectively.

IV.1
Local coherent state of qubits as basic spinor field and local coherent-qubits motion postulate

In general, the qubit basis states can be combined to form high product basis states. In terms of the
real basic qubit-basis

\{\varsigma_{s}\}
in Eq.(
35
) or
complex-conjugated basic qubit-basis

\{\zeta_{s}\}
in Eq.(
37
), we are able to define high product basis states as follows:

\displaystyle\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\equiv\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{n}},\quad\varsigma_{+}=\binom{1}{0},\quad\varsigma_{-}=\binom{0}{1};

(61)

and

\displaystyle\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\equiv\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{n}},\quad\zeta_{+}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{1}{1},\quad\zeta_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{i}{-i},

(62)

with
s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}=\pm
denoting the spin “up
\&
down” states and “n” labeling the qubit number
Q_{N}=n
. Where
\{\varsigma_{s_{i}}\}
and
\{\zeta_{s_{i}}\}
are the real and complex-conjugated basic qubit-bases, respectively. The new bases
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
and
\{\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
are referred to as real and complex-conjugated
n
-product qubit-basis
, respectively. In general, there exist
2^{n}

product qubit-basis states
, which form
2^{n}
-dimensional linear vector space as Hilbert space spanned by
n
-product qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
or
\{\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
.

Let us apply both the real
n
-product qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
and complex-conjugated
n
-product qubit-basis
\{\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
to define
real local coherent state of qubits

\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)
and
self-conjugated local coherent state of qubits

\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{n}}(x)
, respectively, as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{n}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{n}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{n}},

(63)

where the real field operators
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)
are regarded as evolving local distribution amplitudes. As the orthonormal product qubit-basis states span
2^{n}
-dimensional real Hilbert space,
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{n}}(x)
are viewed as vector fields with
2^{n}
local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)
as their components in
2^{n}
-dimensional Hilbert space.

For the simplest case with single qubit number
Q_{N}=1
, we have demonstrated following along the maximum coherence motion principle that the real and complex-conjugated basic qubit-bases bring on correspondingly the real uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
and self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x)
in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, which are shown to be presented as a local coherent state of qubit with two independent degrees of freedom. When extending such a concept to a general case with arbitrary qubit number
Q_{N}=n
, we are motivated to make the following postulate.

Local coherent-qubits motion postulate
: a local coherent state of qubits as a column vector field of local distribution amplitudes obeying the maximum coherence motion principle brings on a basic spinor field which is postulated to be as the basic constituent of matter and referred to as
qubit-spinor field
.

Based on such a local coherent-qubits motion postulate, we may refer to local coherent states of qubits
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{n}}(x)
as
real qubit-spinor field
and
self-conjugated qubit-spinor field
, respectively. For a convention,
qubit-spinor field
will mean
real qubit-spinor field
unless otherwise specified.

We would like to address that local distribution amplitudes of qubit-spinor field are dynamical field operators and their kinematical and dynamical evolutions lead to the laws of nature, which are proposed to be governed by the maximum coherence motion principle and symmetry principle along with the least action principle. It is noted that the local coherent state of qubits as qubit-spinor field is unlike the usual coherent state of qubits in quantum mechanics, where global amplitudes are explained as probability amplitudes of basis states when measuring the qubits according to the Born’s rule.

IV.2
Local coherent states of 2-qubit and 3-qubit in 4D and 8D Hilbert spaces and the appearance of 4D and 6D Minkowski spacetimes for biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields

It is well known that quarks as basic constituents of matter are regarded as Dirac fermions in the strong interaction of SM. In the electromagnetic interaction, both charged leptons and quarks as basic constituents of matter are viewed as Dirac fermions with eight independent degrees of freedom. Neutrinos with four independent degrees of freedom may appear as Majorana fermions in their free motion mass basis. Nevertheless, in the weak interaction, all leptons and quarks behave as Weyl fermions with four independent degrees of freedom. Following along the local coherent-qubits motion postulate, we are going to demonstrate how the leptons and quarks as basic constituents of matter can be characterized by qubit-spinor fields.

Let us begin with examining the simple cases for the product qubit-basis states formed from two qubits
Q_{N}
=2 and three qubits
Q_{N}
=3, which are represented in 4-dimensional and 8-dimensional Hilbert spaces spanned by
2-product qubit-basis states
and
3-product qubit-basis states
, respectively.

In terms of real basic qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s}\}
given in Eq.(
35
), the real product qubit-basis states
\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}
and
\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}
are constructed from the direct tensor product as follows:

\displaystyle\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\equiv\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{2}},\quad\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\equiv\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{3}}\equiv\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{2}s_{3}}=\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{2}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{3}}.

(64)

To be manifest, we write down 2-product qubit-basis states with the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\varsigma_{++}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\varsigma_{+-}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\varsigma_{-+}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
1\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\varsigma_{--}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\\
1\end{pmatrix};

Similarly, one can write down the explicit forms for the
3-product qubit-basis states
.

From the general definition of qubit-spinor field shown in Eq.(
IV.1
), the local coherent states of two qubits and three qubits with real distribution amplitudes can directly be constructed in terms of 2-product qubit-basis states and 3-product qubit-basis states, respectively, as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\equiv\binom{\psi_{Q_{+}^{1}}(x)}{\psi_{Q_{-}^{1}}(x)}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{++}(x)\\
\psi_{+-}(x)\\
\psi_{-+}(x)\\
\psi_{--}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}s_{3}}\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\equiv\binom{\psi_{Q_{+}^{2}}(x)}{\psi_{Q_{-}^{2}}(x)}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{Q_{++}^{1}}(x)\\
\psi_{Q_{+-}^{1}}(x)\\
\psi_{Q_{-+}^{1}}(x)\\
\psi_{Q_{--}^{1}}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{+++}(x)\\
\psi_{++-}(x)\\
\psi_{+-+}(x)\\
\psi_{+--}(x)\\
\psi_{-++}(x)\\
\psi_{-+-}(x)\\
\psi_{--+}(x)\\
\psi_{---}(x)\end{pmatrix},

(65)

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
turn out to be qubit-spinor fields with respective to the vectors in
four-dimensional and eight-dimensional Hilbert spaces
spanned correspondingly by 2-product qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\}
and 3-product qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\}
. Where
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)
(
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm
) correspond to four and eight real local distribution amplitudes,
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)=\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{\ast}(x)
and
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)=\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{\ast}(x)
.

For convenience, the local coherent states of two qubits and three qubits
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
are referred to as
biqubit-spinor field
and
triqubit-spinor field
, respectively, they all have real local distribution amplitudes.

Based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle as guiding principle, we are able to construct the hermitian action of biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)-\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\},

(66)

with
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
and
p=1,2
. Where the real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices read,

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Upsilon^{1}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1},\quad\Upsilon^{3}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(67)

which satisfy Clifford algebra relations shown in Eq.(
II.3
) and characterize the maximally correlated motion of biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

The pure imaginary antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices are found to be,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}^{1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\tilde{\Upsilon}^{2}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(68)

which also satisfy Clifford algebra relations. The number of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices determines the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=2,

(69)

which is the same as qubit number
Q_{N}
.

By applying the maximum coherence motion principle to triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
, we obtain the following hermitian action:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)-\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{3}\phi_{p}(x)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\},

(70)

with
a,\mu=0,1,\cdots,5
and
p=1,2,3
. There are six real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices,

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Upsilon^{1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1},\quad\Upsilon^{3}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{4}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Upsilon^{5}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(71)

which satisfy the Clifford algebra relations shown in Eq.(
II.3
) and characterize the maximally correlated motion of triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

There exist in general three pure imaginary antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}^{1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\tilde{\Upsilon}^{2}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\tilde{\Upsilon}^{3}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(72)

which satisfy Clifford algebra. Nevertheless, it is easy to check that they only anticommute with four of the six real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices as they all commute with the
\Upsilon
-matrix
\Upsilon^{5}
.

It can be verified that the actions presented in Eqs.(
66
) and (
70
) for biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
with zero coupling constant
\lambda_{3}=0
possess the following maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\begin{cases}SP(1,3)\times SP(2)\cong SO(1,3)\times SO(2),&\;$for$\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),\\
SP(1,5)\times SP(3)\cong SO(1,5)\times SO(3),&\;$for$\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x).\end{cases}

(73)

The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
is the same as the qubit number
Q_{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=3.

(74)

It is noted that for the case with non-zero coupling constant
\lambda_{3}\neq 0
, the action given in Eq. (
70
) for triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
has only a reduced symmetry,

\displaystyle G_{S}=SP(1,4)\times SP(3)\cong SO(1,4)\times SO(3),\;\;\text{for}\;\;\lambda_{3}\neq 0.

This is because the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrix
\Upsilon^{5}
presented in Eq.(
IV.2
) commutes with all three antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
72
), which do not meet to Clifford algebra.

To obey the maximum coherence motion principle and preserve the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5), the action of triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
is simplified to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x),

(75)

with
a,\mu=0,1,\cdots,5
and
\lambda_{3}=0
.

Therefore, the local coherent states of 2-qubit and 3-qubit as biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
can reach maximally correlated motions in four-dimensional and six-dimensional Minkowski spacetimes, respectively. It is shown explicitly how the genesis of spacetime dimension is correlated to the local coherent state of qubits by following along the maximum coherence motion principle as guiding principle.

IV.3
Dynamics of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field and intrinsic discrete symmetries of biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields

To show symmetry properties of the action presented in Eq.(
66
), it is useful to rewrite it into the conventional form in terms of
\gamma
-matrices and consider scalar fields as dynamical fields. In general, we arrive at the following action for the biqubit-spinor field with scalar fields as dynamical fields in four-dimensional spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)-\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)

(76)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{p}(x)\partial_{\nu}\phi^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{Q}(\phi_{p}(x)\phi^{p}(x))^{2}\},

with
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
and
p=1,2
. Where we have adopted the conventional definition
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\dagger}(x)\gamma^{0}
and introduced
\gamma
-matrices with the following explicit structures:

\displaystyle\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3},\quad\gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}^{1}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\tilde{\gamma}^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(77)

where the
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}\equiv\tilde{\Upsilon}^{2}\Upsilon^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) are all imaginary ones and the
\tilde{\gamma}
-matrices
\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\equiv\tilde{\Upsilon}^{2}\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p}
(
p=1,2
) are all real ones. It is noted that
\tilde{\gamma}^{2}
commutes with all other
\gamma
-matrices.

The presence of two scalar fields
\phi_{p}(x)
(p=1,2) is associated with two
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices characterized by
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{Q_{c}}=2
. For convenience, such two scalar fields
\phi_{p}(x)
(p=1,2) are referred to as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field
.

In analogous, the action in Eq.(
75
) can be written into the following conventional form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)

(78)

with
\hat{a}=(a,5,6)
,
\hat{\mu}=(\mu,5,6)
(
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
) and
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\dagger}(x)\Gamma^{0}
. The explicit forms of
\Gamma
-matrices are found to be,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{1}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1},\quad\Gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{6}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(79)

with
\Gamma^{a}=\tilde{\Upsilon}^{3}\Upsilon^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) and
\Gamma^{a}=\tilde{\Upsilon}^{3}\Upsilon^{(a-1)}
(
a=5,6
).
Where all
\gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
are imaginary ones. Unlike the usual case, the matrix
\Gamma^{6}
becomes commuting with all other
\Gamma
-matrices.

Let us now discuss intrinsic discrete symmetries of the actions under the operations: charge-conjugation (
\mathcal{C}
), parity-inversion (
\mathcal{P}
), time-reversal (
\mathcal{T}
). To preserve the invariance of the action in Eq.(
76
) under
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{T}
operations, the biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field
\phi_{p}(x)
(
p=1,2
) should transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{c}(x)=C_{4}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{T}(x),

\displaystyle C_{4}^{-1}\gamma^{a}C_{4}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\quad C_{4}=\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle C_{4}^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}^{p}C_{4}=\tilde{\gamma}^{p\,T},\quad\mathcal{C}\phi_{p}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}=\phi_{p}(x),

for the charge conjugation, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{p}(x)=P_{4}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x^{0},-x^{k}),

\displaystyle P_{4}^{-1}\gamma^{a}P_{4}=\gamma^{a\,\dagger},\quad P_{4}=-i\gamma^{0}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle P_{4}^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}^{p}P_{4}=\tilde{\gamma}^{p\,\dagger},\quad\mathcal{P}\phi_{p}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}=\begin{cases}-\phi_{p}(x^{0},-x^{k}),\;p=1,\\
+\phi_{p}(x^{0},-x^{k}),\;p=2,\end{cases}

for the parity-inversion, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{t}(x)=T_{4}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(-x^{0},x^{k}),

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-1}\gamma^{a}T_{4}=\gamma^{a\,T},\quad T_{4}=i\gamma^{1}\gamma^{2}\gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}^{p}T_{4}=\tilde{\gamma}^{p\,T},\quad\mathcal{T}\phi_{p}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}=\begin{cases}-\phi_{p}(-x^{0},x^{k}),\;p=1,\\
+\phi_{p}(-x^{0},x^{k}),\;p=2,\end{cases}

for the time-reversal.

It is seen that the biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
obeys conventional transformation properties under the well-defined discrete symmetries of charge-conjugation (
\mathcal{C}
), parity-inversion (
\mathcal{P}
) and time-reversal (
\mathcal{T}
). For the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field
\phi_{p}(x)
(
p=1,2
),
\phi_{1}(x)
transforms as pseudoscalar field and
\phi_{2}(x)
as scalar field. The combined joint operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
on biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field in 4-dimensional spacetime leads to the following conventional transformations:

\displaystyle\varTheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{T}(-x),\quad\Theta^{-1}\gamma^{a}\Theta=-\gamma^{a\,\dagger},\quad\Theta\gamma^{0}=-\gamma^{0}\Theta,

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\Theta=\tilde{\gamma}^{p\,\dagger},\quad\varTheta\phi_{p}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\phi_{p}(-x).

To ensure the invariance of the action in Eq.(
78
) under
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{T}
operations, the triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
is found to transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{6}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{T}(x),

\displaystyle C_{6}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}C_{6}=-\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,T},\quad C_{6}=\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

for the charge conjugation, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{p}(x)=P_{6}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x^{0},-x^{k},x^{6}),

\displaystyle P_{6}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}P_{6}=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,\dagger},\quad P_{6}=-i\Gamma^{0}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

for the parity-inversion, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{t_{6}}(x)=T_{6}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(-x^{0},x^{k},-x^{6}),

\displaystyle T_{6}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}T_{6}=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,T},\quad T_{6}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

for the time-reversal.

It is noticed that the spatial coordinate
x^{6}
has a distinguished transformation feature under both parity-inversion and time-reversal operations as it has to undergo a flip in sign in order to keep the invariance of the action. For that, it is useful to consider the combined operator
\vartheta\equiv\mathcal{PT}
, which leads to a proper transformation as the usual one,

\displaystyle\vartheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\vartheta^{-1}=\theta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(-x),\quad\theta^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\theta=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,\ast}.

For the combined joint operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
on the triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
in 6-dimensional spacetime, we arrive at the conventional transformation property as follows:

\displaystyle\varTheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{T}(-x),\quad\Theta^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\Theta=-\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,\dagger},\quad\Theta\gamma^{0}=-\gamma^{0}\Theta,

which indicates that both the combined operator
\vartheta
and combined joint operator
\varTheta
become essential ones for the triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
in 6-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

The real property of
qubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
can be characterized by their self-conjugate conditions,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{c}(x)=C_{4}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),\quad C_{4}=\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{6}\Gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x),\quad C_{6}=\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(80)

with
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{c}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{c}(x)
defined as charge conjugations of biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
in 4-dimensional and 6-dimensional spacetimes, respectively.

IV.4
Maximal coherent symmetries of biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields

Let us further analyze invariant properties of the actions presented in Eqs.(
76
) and (
78
) under transformations of maximal coherent symmetries.

It can be verified that the action in Eq.(
76
) keeps invariant under the following transformations for biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field
\phi_{p}(x)
as well as coordinates of Minkowski spacetime:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=S(L)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),\quad\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x^{\prime})=\phi_{p}(x),

\displaystyle S^{-1}(L)\gamma^{a}S(L)=L^{a}_{\;b}\gamma^{b},\quad x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu},\quad(a,b,\mu,\nu=0,1,2,3),

\displaystyle S(L)=e^{i\Sigma^{ab}\varpi_{ab}}\in SP(1,3),\quad L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}=L^{a}_{\;b}\in SO(1,3),

for
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, and

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\prime}(x)=e^{i\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}\,\varpi_{pq}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),\quad\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x)=L_{p}^{\;\,q}\phi_{q}(x),

\displaystyle e^{i\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}\,\varpi_{pq}}\in SP(2),\quad L_{p}^{\;\,q}\in SO(2),\quad(p,q=1,2),

for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, and

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{\frac{3}{2}\varpi}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),\quad\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x^{\prime})=e^{\varpi}\phi_{p}(x),

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=e^{-\varpi}x^{\mu},\quad e^{\frac{3}{2}\varpi},e^{\pm\varpi}\in SC(1)\cong SG(1),

for global scaling symmetry. Where
\Sigma^{ab}
are the group generators of SP(1,3) and
\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}
the group generator of SP(2), which can be defined from the
\gamma
-matrices given in Eq.(
IV.3
) and
\tilde{\Upsilon}
-matrices defined in Eq.(
68
),

\displaystyle\Sigma^{ab}=\frac{i}{4}[\gamma^{a},\gamma^{b}],\quad\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p},\tilde{\Upsilon}^{q}]=\frac{i}{2}\tilde{\Upsilon}^{1}\tilde{\Upsilon}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}.

The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin bi-scalar field
\phi_{p}(x)
(
p=1,2
) can be regarded as a vector field under SO(2) transformation.

After considering translation group symmetry
P^{1,3}
in 4-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, we arrive at a maximal associated symmetry of the action given in Eq.(
76
),

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,3}\ltimes SO(1,3)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,3)\times SG(1)\times SP(2)

(81)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,3)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,3)\times SG(1)\times SP(2),

with PO(1,3)
\equiv
P
{}^{1,3}\ltimes
SO(1,3) denoting Poincaré group symmetry in 4-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. Note that the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
” is adopted to indicate the associated symmetry in which the transformations of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,3) and global scaling symmetry SG(1) in Hilbert space have to be coincidental to those of Lorentz group symmetry SO(1,3) and conformal scaling symmetry SC(1) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime.

Similarly, it can be checked that the action in Eq.(
78
) becomes invariant under the following transformations for triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
and coordinates in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=S(L)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x),\quad x^{\hat{\mu}}\to x^{\prime\hat{\mu}}=L^{\mu}_{\;\hat{\nu}}x^{\hat{\nu}},

\displaystyle S^{-1}(L)\Gamma^{\hat{a}}S(L)=L^{\hat{a}}_{\;\hat{b}}\Gamma^{\hat{b}},\quad\hat{a},\hat{b},\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu}=0,1,2,3,5,6,

\displaystyle S(L)=e^{i\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}\varpi_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}}\in SP(1,5),\quad L^{\hat{\mu}}_{\;\hat{\nu}}=L^{\hat{a}}_{\;\hat{b}}\in SO(1,5),

for maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, and

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\prime}(x)=e^{i\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}\,\varpi_{pq}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x),

\displaystyle e^{i\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}\,\varpi_{pq}}\in SP(3)\cong SO(3),\quad p,q=1,2,3,

for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, and

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{\frac{5}{2}\varpi}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x),

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=e^{-\varpi}x^{\mu},\quad e^{\frac{5}{2}\varpi},e^{-\varpi}\in SC(1)\cong SG(1),

for global scaling symmetry. Where
\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
are the group generators of SP(1,5) and
\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}
the group generators of SP(3), they are defined correspondingly from
\gamma
-matrices presented in Eq.(
IV.3
) and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices in Eq.(
72
),

\displaystyle\Sigma^{ab}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{b}],\quad\Sigma^{a6}=-\Sigma^{6a}=\frac{i}{2}\Gamma^{a}\Gamma^{6},\quad(a,b=0,1,2,3,5),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\Upsilon}^{p},\tilde{\Upsilon}^{q}],\quad p,q=1,2,3.

It is noted that the
\Gamma
-matrix
\Gamma^{6}
is not anti-commuting with all other
\Gamma
-matrices.

By including the translation group symmetry
P^{1,5}
and global scaling symmetry in 6-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, the action in Eq.(
78
) possesses the following maximal associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,5}\ltimes SO(1,5)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,5)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(3)

(82)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,5)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,5)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(3),

where PO(1,5)
\equiv
P
{}^{1,5}\ltimes
SO(1,5) represents Poincaré-type group symmetry in 6-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

It is noticed that the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5) does not allow to have scalar coupling term between triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
\phi_{p}(x)
(
p=1,2,3
), i.e.,
\lambda_{3}=0
, as such a scalar coupling term will spoil maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5). The reason is that the three antisymmetric
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
72
) are not anti-commuting with all real symmetric
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{a}
(
a=0,1\cdots,5
) defined in Eq.(
IV.2
). Specifically, it is the real symmetric
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrix
\Upsilon^{5}
that commutes with all three antisymmetric
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices, so that the introduction of scalar coupling term (
\lambda_{3}\neq 0
) will bring the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5) broken down to the reduced
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,4).

IV.5
Local coherent states of 2-qubit and 3-qubit as basic constituents of matter and Majorana and Dirac fermions as biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields

The neutrino oscillation indicates that the freely moving neutrinos may appear as Majorana fermions with small masses and large mixings. In the electromagnetic interaction, the charged leptons and quarks behave as Dirac fermions with eight independent degrees of freedom, which are described by the complex four-component entity spinor fields. We will show that Majorana and Dirac fermions as basic constituents of matter can be viewed as local coherent states of qubits in correspondence to biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
. In particular, we will demonstrate explicitly the relationship between the complex and self-conjugated spinor representations of qubit-spinor field.

To express the biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
into the usual Majorana and Dirac fermions composed of complex components in the spinor representation, it is useful to apply the complex-conjugated basic qubit-basis
\{\zeta_{s}\}
defined in Eq.(
37
) to form the complex-conjugated
product qubit-basis states

\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}}
and
\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}
with the following forms:

\displaystyle\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\zeta_{s_{2}},\quad\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}=\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\otimes\zeta_{s_{3}}=\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\zeta_{s_{2}s_{3}}=\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\zeta_{s_{2}}\otimes\zeta_{s_{3}}.

(83)

For an illustration, let us write down the explicit forms for the complex-conjugated
2-product qubit-basis states

\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta_{++}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\\
1\\
1\end{pmatrix},\quad\zeta_{+-}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}i\\
-i\\
i\\
-i\end{pmatrix},\quad\zeta_{-+}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}i\\
i\\
-i\\
-i\end{pmatrix},\quad\zeta_{--}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
1\\
1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}.

In a similar way, one can directly write down the complex-conjugated
3-product qubit-basis states

\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}
.

In light of the complex-conjugated
2-product qubit-basis

\{\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\}
and
3-product qubit-basis

\{\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\}
, the local coherent states of qubits for biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
can equivalently be represented by the corresponding
self-cojugated biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
, respectively. Explicitly, they can be expressed as the following forms:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}}\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}},

(84)

where
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)
are real local distribution amplitudes.

In light of self-conjugated biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
, we are able to express the actions given in Eqs.(
76
) and (
78
) into the following equivalent ones:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)

(85)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{p}(x)\partial_{\nu}\phi^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{Q}(\phi_{p}(x)\phi^{p}(x))^{2}\},

for the action of
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and
\phi_{p}(x)
with the definition
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}^{\dagger}(x)\gamma^{0}
. Where the
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) including
\tilde{\gamma}^{p}
(
p=1,2
) have the following structures:

\displaystyle\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3},\quad\gamma^{1}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1},\quad\gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}^{1}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}=i\gamma_{5},\quad\tilde{\gamma}^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}.

(86)

And similarly we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

(87)

for the action of
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
with the definition
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{\dagger}(x)\Gamma^{0}
. The
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
(
\hat{a}=0,1,2,3,5,6
) have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3},\quad\Gamma^{1}=-i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1},\quad\Gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(88)

and three
\tilde{\Gamma}
-matrices characterizing
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge are given by,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{1}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\tilde{\Gamma}^{2}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\tilde{\Gamma}^{3}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}.

(89)

It is noticed that not all
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
and
\tilde{\gamma}
-matrices
\tilde{\gamma}^{p}
become anti-commuting since
\tilde{\gamma}^{2}
is actually commuting with all others. Analogously, the
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}
-matrices
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
are not all anti-commuting as the matrix
\Gamma^{5}
commutes with other
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
and the matrix
\tilde{\Gamma}^{3}
is commuting with all others.

Both
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
retain the same independent degrees of freedom as biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}}(x)
, respectively, they all satisfy the following self-conjugate conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}\Gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}=-\gamma^{1}\gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\equiv\sigma_{3}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}},

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\equiv\sigma_{1}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}},

(90)

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}^{c}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{c}(x)
are defined as the charge-conjugations in 4-dimensional and 6-dimensional Minkowski spacetimes, respectively.

In fact, the self-conjugated biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
defines the well-known Majorana fermion
\psi_{M}(x)
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\psi_{M}(x),

which was introduced initially in 4-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

In such self-conjugated qubit-spinor representations, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries for local coherent states of qubits as self-conjugated quibt-spinor fields can explicitly be characterized as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)=e^{i\varpi_{12}\tilde{\Sigma}^{12}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x),\quad e^{i\varpi_{12}\tilde{\Sigma}^{12}}\in SP(2)\cong U(1),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=e^{i\varpi_{ij}\tilde{\varSigma}^{ij}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),\quad e^{i\varpi_{ij}\tilde{\varSigma}^{ij}}\in SP(3)\cong SU(2),

(91)

with
\tilde{\Sigma}^{12}
the U(1) group generator and
\tilde{\varSigma}^{ij}
the SU(2) group generators,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}^{12}=-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varSigma}^{12}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{3}\times\sigma_{0}\times\sigma_{0}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{7},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varSigma}^{23}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{1}\times\gamma_{5},\quad\tilde{\varSigma}^{31}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{2}\times\gamma_{5},

(92)

which indicates that their
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges are the same as qubit numbers, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}
.

When rewriting self-conjugated biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
by utilizing the real
2-product qubit-basis

\{\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\}
and
3-product qubit-basis

\{\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\}
defined in Eq.(
64
), we obtain the explicit expressions for such two self-conjugated qubit-spinor fields with complex local distribution amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\psi}_{++}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{+-}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{-+}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{--}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\equiv\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\psi}_{+++}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{++-}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{+-+}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{+--}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{-++}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{-+-}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{--+}(x)\\
\tilde{\psi}_{---}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)},

(93)

where
\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)
and
\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}(x)
are complex local distribution amplitudes.

In the above expressions, we have introduced both
complex uniqubit-spinor field

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
complex biqubit-spinor field

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
and defined correspondingly their
complex charge-conjugated fields

\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
, which can generally be expressed by adopting real product qubit-basis states with complex local distribution amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\equiv\sum_{s_{1}=\pm}\hat{\psi}_{s_{1}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}}=\binom{\hat{\psi}_{+}(x)}{\hat{\psi}_{-}(x)}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\equiv\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)=\binom{\hat{\psi}_{-}^{\ast}(x)}{\hat{\psi}_{+}^{\ast}(x)}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}^{\ast}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}^{\ast}(x)},

(94)

for the complex uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and its complex charge-conjugated one
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
. There are following relations between complex and real distribution amplitudes:

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{+}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{++}(x)=\frac{1}{2}[\psi_{++}(x)+i\psi_{+-}(x)+i\psi_{-+}(x)-\psi_{--}(x)],

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{+0}+i\psi_{+1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{-}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{+-}(x)=\frac{1}{2}[\psi_{++}(x)-i\psi_{+-}(x)+i\psi_{-+}(x)+\psi_{--}(x)],

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{-0}+i\psi_{-1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{-}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{-+}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{+-}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{0}}^{\ast}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{+}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{--}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{++}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{0}}^{\ast}(x).

Similarly, the complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
and its complex charge-conjugated one
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}}\hat{\psi}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\psi}_{++}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{+-}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{-+}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{--}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{1}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{1}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\ast}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\psi}_{--}^{\ast}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{-+}^{\ast}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{+-}^{\ast}(x)\\
\hat{\psi}_{++}^{\ast}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}--}}^{\ast}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}^{\ast}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}^{\ast}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}++}}^{\ast}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{1}}(x)}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{1}}(x)},

(95)

where the relations between complex and real local distribution amplitudes are given by,

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{++}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{+++}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}[\psi_{+++}(x)+i\psi_{++-}(x)+i\psi_{+-+}(x)-\psi_{+--}(x)
