\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\psi_{-++}(x)-\psi_{-+-}(x)-\psi_{--+}(x)-i\psi_{---}(x)]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{++0}+i\psi_{++1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{+-}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{++-}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}[\psi_{+++}(x)-i\psi_{++-}(x)+i\psi_{+-+}(x)+\psi_{+--}(x)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\psi_{-++}(x)+\psi_{-+-}(x)-\psi_{--+}(x)+i\psi_{---}(x)]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{+-0}+i\psi_{+-1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{-+}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{+-+}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}[\psi_{+++}(x)+i\psi_{++-}(x)-i\psi_{+-+}(x)+\psi_{+--}(x)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\psi_{-++}(x)-\psi_{-+-}(x)+\psi_{--+}(x)+i\psi_{---}(x)]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{-+0}+i\psi_{-+1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{--}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{+--}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}[\psi_{+++}(x)-i\psi_{++-}(x)-i\psi_{+-+}(x)-\psi_{+--}(x)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\psi_{-++}(x)+\psi_{-+-}(x)+\psi_{--+}(x)-i\psi_{---}(x)]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\psi_{--0}+i\psi_{--1}]\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x),

and

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{--}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{-++}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{+--}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}--}}^{\ast}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{-+}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{-+-}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{+-+}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}^{\ast}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{+-}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{--+}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{++-}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}^{\ast}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{++}^{\ast}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{---}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{+++}^{\ast}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\mathsf{C}++}}^{\ast}(x).

In general, there exist relations between the complex qubit-spinor field and its qubit-reduced complex qubit-spinor field. In particular, with the introduction of complex charge-conjugated qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
, the self-conjugated biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
can be characterized in formal by the corresponding qubit-reduced complex qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
defined via the
reduced product qubit-basis states
with complex local distribution amplitudes. We may refer to complex qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
as complex uniqubit-spinor field and complex biqubit-spinor field, respectively.

In fact, the complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
with four complex components behaves as a Dirac fermion,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\to\psi_{D}(x).

Note that the ordinary Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
was introduced initially by Dirac in 4-dimensional Minkowski spacetime as it was assumed to be a massive spinor field. Therefore, it is distinguished to massive Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
, a complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
as massless Dirac fermion possesses generally a free motion in 6-dimensional Minkowski spacetime
GGFT6D
based on the maximum coherence motion principle.

In terms of the complex qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
, the actions given in Eqs.(
85
) and (
87
) can be rewritten into equivalent formalisms. For the complex uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
, we obtain the following hermitian action:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{[\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)+H.c.]

(96)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{p}(x)\partial_{\nu}\phi^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{Q}(\phi_{p}(x)\phi^{p}(x))^{2}\},

with the definitions
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{\dagger}(x)\gamma^{0}
and
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)=\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{\ast}(x)
as shown in Eq.(
IV.5
). The
\gamma
-matrices
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) and
\tilde{\gamma}^{p}
(
p=1,2
) are explicitly given by,

\displaystyle\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3},\quad\gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{2},\quad\gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{1},\quad\gamma^{3}=-\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}^{1}=\sigma_{0},\quad\tilde{\gamma}^{2}=-i\sigma_{0},

(97)

which are defined in two-dimensional complex Hilbert space.

For the massless complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
, we arrive at the following hermitian action in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{D}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)+H.c.],

(98)

with the definition
\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\dagger}(x)\Gamma^{0}
. The
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
(
\hat{a}=0,1,2,3,5,6
) are found to have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3},\quad\Gamma^{1}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\Gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{5}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(99)

where
\Gamma
-matrices and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
are represented in four-dimensional complex Hilbert space.

IV.6
Intrinsic properties of complex uniqubit-spinor and complex biqubit-spinor fields with SL(2,
\mathbb{C}
) and SL(2,
\mathbb{Q}
) group symmetries in 4D and 6D Minkowski spacetimes

In light of the complex uniqubit-spinor and biqubit-spinor fields, the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry of the action in Eq.(
96
) is described by a special linear group of degree 2 over the complex number
\mathbb{C}
, i.e., SL(2,
\mathbb{C}
), which has the following transformation properties:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x^{\prime})=e^{i\hat{\varpi}_{k}\sigma^{k}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x),\;(k=1,2,3),

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}x^{\nu},\quad e^{-i\hat{\varpi}_{k}^{\ast}\sigma^{k}}\gamma^{a}e^{i\hat{\varpi}_{k}\sigma^{k}}=L^{a}_{\;b}\gamma^{b},

\displaystyle e^{i\hat{\varpi}_{k}\sigma^{k}}\in SL(2,\mathbb{C})\cong SP(1,3),

\displaystyle L^{a}_{\;b}=L^{\mu}_{\;\nu}\in SO(1,3)\cong SL(2,\mathbb{C}),

(100)

where
\hat{\varpi}_{k}
(
k=1,2,3
) are three complex parameters and
\sigma^{k}
are Pauli matrices, which indicates the following isomorphism of symmetry groups:

\displaystyle SL(2,\mathbb{C})\cong SP(1,3)\cong SO(1,3).

(101)

The special linear group SL(2,
\mathbb{C}
) reflects the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry of complex uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
with two complex local distribution amplitudes. Such an isomorphism of symmetry groups brings on the equivalence between complex uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and Majorana fermion
\psi_{M}(x)
which is regarded as a self-conjugated biqubit-spinor field with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,3).

The
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry of the action in Eq.(
98
) is described by another special linear group of degree 2 over the
quaternion

\mathbb{Q}
, i.e., SL(2,
\mathbb{Q}
), which is isomorphic to special unitary group
SU^{\ast}(4)
under the following transformations:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x^{\prime})=S(L)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x),

\displaystyle x^{\hat{\mu}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\hat{\mu}}=L^{\hat{\mu}}_{\;\hat{\nu}}x^{\hat{\nu}},\quad S^{-1}(L)\Gamma^{\hat{a}}S(L)=L^{\hat{a}}_{\;\hat{b}}\,\Gamma^{\hat{b}},

\displaystyle S(L)=e^{i\varpi_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}}\in SL(2,\mathbb{Q})\cong SU^{\ast}(4)\cong SP(1,5),

\displaystyle L^{\hat{a}}_{\;\hat{b}}=L^{\hat{\mu}}_{\;\hat{\nu}}\in SO(1,5)\cong SP(1,5),

where the fifteen
4\times 4
matrices
\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
(
\hat{a},\hat{b}=0,\cdots,3,5,6
) defined via the
\Gamma
-matrices in Eq.(
IV.5
) provide the group generators of
SL(2,\mathbb{Q})\cong SU^{\ast}(4)
symmetry in the spinor representation,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{ab}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{b}]=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{b}],

\displaystyle\Sigma^{a5}=-\Sigma^{5a}=\frac{i}{2}\Gamma^{a},\quad a,b=0,1,2,3,6.

(102)

We have the following isomorphic property of symmetry groups,

\displaystyle SL(2,\mathbb{Q})\cong SU^{\ast}(4)\cong SP(1,5)\cong SO(1,5),

(103)

which indicates that the maximal special unitary symmetry of complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
with four complex local distribution amplitudes is equivalent to the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5) of self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
with four pairs of complex-conjugated local distribution amplitudes.

Therefore, a massless complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
possesses the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry
SL(2,\mathbb{Q})\cong SU^{\ast}(4)\cong SP(1,5)\cong SO(1,5)
in six-dimensional spacetime
GGFT6D
instead of the ordinary symmetry
SP(1,3)\cong SO(1,3)
for massive Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
in four-dimensional spacetime.

We now turn to discuss the basic properties of the actions presented in Eqs.(
96
) and (
98
) under intrinsic discrete symmetries: parity-inversion (
\mathcal{P}
), time-reversal (
\mathcal{T}
), charge-conjugation (
\mathcal{C}
) and W-parity operation (
\mathcal{W}
). The complex qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
in four- and six-dimensional spacetimes transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{p}(x)=P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x^{0},-x^{1},-x^{2},x^{3})\,,

\displaystyle P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{-1}\gamma^{a}P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=\gamma^{a\,\dagger},\quad P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3},\quad a=0,\cdots,3,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{p}(x)=P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x^{0},-x^{i},x^{5},-x^{6})\,,\quad i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,\dagger},\quad P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{0},\quad\hat{a}=0,\cdots,3,5,6,

for the parity-inversion, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{T}(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},-x^{3})\,,

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{-1}\gamma^{a}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\;\;a=0,1,2,\quad C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{-1}\gamma^{3}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=\gamma^{2\,T},

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=-i\gamma^{0}\gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{T}(x^{\mu},-x^{5},-x^{6}),\;\;\mu=0,1,2,3,

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{a}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=-\Gamma^{a\,T},\quad C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{k}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{k\,T},\;\;a=0,1,2,3,\;k=5,6,

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{2}\Gamma^{6}=-i\sigma_{3}\times\sigma_{2},

(104)

for the charge conjugation, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{t}(x)=T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(-x^{0},x^{i}),\;\;i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{-1}\gamma^{a}T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\;\;a=0,1,2,\quad T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{-1}\gamma^{3}T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=\gamma^{3\,T},

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}=\gamma^{1}\gamma^{3}=-i\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{t}(x)=T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(-x^{0},x^{i},-x^{5},x^{6}),\;\;i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,T},\quad T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{1}\Gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{3}\times\sigma_{2},\quad\hat{a}=0,\cdots,3,5,6,

for the time-reversal, and

\displaystyle\varTheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}^{T}(-x),

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\gamma^{a}\Theta=\gamma^{a\,\dagger},\quad\Theta=CPT=\gamma^{0},

\displaystyle\varTheta\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{T}(-x^{\mu},x^{5},x^{6}),\mu=0,\cdots,3,

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\Gamma^{a}\Theta=-\Gamma^{a\,\dagger},\;\;a=0,\cdots,3,

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\Gamma^{k}\Theta=\Gamma^{k\,\dagger},\;\;k=5,6,\quad\Theta=CPT=\Gamma^{0},

(105)

for the combined operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
.

It is noticed that for the complex uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)
, there are opposite transformation properties for the third coordinate
x^{3}
under parity-inversion
\mathcal{P}
and charge-conjugation
\mathcal{C}
, but their combined operation
\mathcal{CP}
becomes the same as ordinary combined one in four-dimensional spacetime. For the complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
, there appear different transformation properties for coordinate
x^{5}
under parity-inversion
\mathcal{P}
and time-reversal
\mathcal{T}
, while their combined operation
\mathcal{PT}
comes to be the same as usual combined one in six-dimensional spacetime. Nevertheless, the combined operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
on the complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
does not bring on a proper transformation for all six coordinates in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, which is caused from an unusual transformation feature of charge-conjugation operation under which two extra dimensions corresponding to coordinates
x^{5}
and
x^{6}
have to flip in sign in order to ensure the invariance of the action.

It is useful to define the so-called W-parity operation discussed in
GGFT6D
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{w}(x)=W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x^{\mu}-x^{5},-x^{6}),

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{a}W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=-\Gamma^{a},\;\;a=0,1,2,3,\;\;\mu=0,1,2,3,

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{-1}\Gamma^{k}W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=\Gamma^{k},\;\;k=5,6,\quad W_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=i\gamma_{5},

(106)

which motivates us to introduce the combined operation
\mathcal{\tilde{C}}={\cal W}{\cal C}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{C}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\mathcal{\tilde{C}}^{-1}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\tilde{c}}(x)=\tilde{C}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{T}(x),

\displaystyle\left(\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\tilde{c}}(x)\right)^{\tilde{c}}=-\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x),\quad\tilde{C}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=i\gamma_{5}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}=-\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(107)

so that all six-dimensional coordinates become unchanged. Such a combined operation
\mathcal{\tilde{C}}={\cal W}{\cal C}
is referred to as
W-charge conjugation
.

When applying the joint operator
\tilde{\varTheta}\equiv\mathcal{\tilde{C}PT}=\mathcal{WCPT}
to massless complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, we obtain the following proper transformation:

\displaystyle\tilde{\varTheta}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\tilde{\varTheta}^{-1}=\tilde{\Theta}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{T}(-x),\quad\tilde{\Theta}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\tilde{\Theta}=\Gamma^{\hat{a}\,\dagger},

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}=\tilde{C}PT=WCPT=i\gamma_{5}\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3},

(108)

which indicates that the joint operation
\tilde{\varTheta}
instead of the usual joint operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
becomes an essential one for complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
as massless Dirac spinor field in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

IV.7
Massless Dirac fermion with W-charge conjugation in 6D Minkowski spacetime and self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field with intrinsic
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SU(2)

The hermitian action in Eq.(
98
) with the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry
SL(2,\mathbb{Q})\cong SU^{\ast}(4)\cong SP(1,5)
can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)+H.c.]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\tilde{c}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\tilde{c}}(x)],

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}^{\tilde{c}}(x)
is called
W-charge conjugated complex biqubit-spinor field
defined in Eq.(
IV.6
) with the introduction of W-parity operation.

As the structure of
\Gamma
-matrices presented in Eq.(
IV.5
) for the action of complex biqubit-spinor
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)
in Eq.(
98
) differs from that of widely used
\gamma
-matrices for the ordinary massive Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
, let us make the following unitary transformation:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)=U_{D}\psi_{D}(x),\quad U_{D}=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{0}&0\\
0&i\sigma_{3}\end{pmatrix},

(109)

which brings on the usual spinor representation for Dirac fermion. The hermitian action can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{D}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{D}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{D}(x)+H.c.]

(110)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{D}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{D}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)],

with the definition
\bar{\psi}_{D}(x)=\psi_{D}^{\dagger}(x)\Gamma^{0}
. Where
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\equiv(\gamma^{a},\Gamma^{l})
(
a=0,1,2,3
,
l=5,6
) get the widely used structures in the spinor representation,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}\equiv\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{1}\equiv\gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}\equiv\gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},\quad\Gamma^{3}\equiv\gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}\equiv i\gamma_{5}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},\quad\Gamma^{6}\equiv I_{4}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(111)

where
\Gamma^{a}=\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3,5
) provide the usual Dirac
\gamma
-matrices in chiral representation. It is manifest that the unit matrix
\Gamma^{6}
is not anti-commuting with others.

Such a unitary transformed complex biqubit-spinor field leads to a spinor structure for an ordinary massless Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
.
\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)
defines a W-charge conjugated Dirac fermion,

\displaystyle\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)\equiv\mathcal{\tilde{C}}\psi_{D}(x)\mathcal{\tilde{C}}^{-1}=\tilde{C}_{D}\bar{\psi}_{D}^{T}(x),

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{D}=\Gamma^{2}\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{6}\gamma_{5}=i\gamma_{5}C_{D}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle C_{D}\equiv C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\equiv C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}=i\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{2}\Gamma^{6}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(112)

In general, a massless Dirac fermion possesses the maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry
SL(2,\mathbb{Q})
, which indicates that the quaternions should be applicable to describe a massless Dirac fermion in six-dimensional spacetime.

In terms of the massless Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
and its W-charge conjugated one
\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)
, the action in Eq.(
110
) can be rewritten into the following simple form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

(113)

where
\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
is referred to as
self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
defined as follows in six-dimensional spacetime:

\displaystyle\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\equiv\binom{\psi_{D}(x)}{\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{T}(x)=\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

\displaystyle\left(\psi_{D}^{\tilde{c}}(x)\right)^{\tilde{c}}=-\psi_{D}(x),\quad C_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\tilde{C}_{D},

(114)

which is related to the previous defined self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
via the following unitary transformation:

\displaystyle\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=U_{M}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),\quad U_{M}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{D}^{\dagger}&0\\
0&U_{D}\end{pmatrix},

(115)

where we have used the tilted bold letter “
\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}
” to distinguish different representations between two self-conjugated triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
. While the
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
appearing in Eq.(
113
) should have the same structure as those in Eq.(
IV.7
) but with a representation in eight-dimensional Hilbert space.

The action in Eq.(
113
) possesses explicitly the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(3)
\cong
SU(2) with the following group generators:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}^{ij}=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\Gamma}^{i},\tilde{\Gamma}^{j}]=-\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{ijk}\tilde{\Gamma}^{k}=\epsilon^{ijk}\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{k}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

which reflects a charge-spin symmetry between Dirac fermion and its W-charge conjugated one. In such a self-conjugated representation, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge becomes manifest and equals to the qubit number, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=3
.

Let us introduce further an alternative structure for
self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
by adopting massless Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
together with its complex charge conjugated one
\psi_{D}^{\bar{c}}(x)
instead of W-charge conjugated one. Explicitly, we define such an alternative self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\equiv\binom{\psi_{D}(x)}{\psi_{D}^{\bar{c}}(x)},\quad\psi_{D}^{\bar{c}}(x)\equiv C_{D}\bar{\psi}^{T}_{D}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{T}(x)=\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),\quad C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes C_{D}.

(116)

The corresponding action is found to be,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

(117)

where
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\equiv(\gamma^{a},\Gamma^{l})
(
a=0,1,2,3
,
l=5,6
) get a different structure. Their explicit forms are given as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{a}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\gamma^{a},\quad a=0,1,2,3,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5},\quad\Gamma^{6}=\sigma_{3}\otimes I_{4},

(118)

where
\gamma^{a}
are usual Dirac
\gamma
-matrices defined in Eq.(
IV.7
). The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin matrices
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
(
p=1,2,3
) are found to be,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{1}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\gamma_{5},\quad\tilde{\Gamma}^{2}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\gamma_{5},\quad\tilde{\Gamma}^{3}=\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{4}.

(119)

Actually, in any representation of self-conjugated qubit-spinor field, all
\Gamma
-matrices should be constructed to meet the following general conditions under charge-conjugation operation:

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=-\Gamma^{\hat{a}T},\quad C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}^{-1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{pT}.

(120)

It can be verified that
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
matrices presented in Eq.(
IV.7
) and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
matrices given in Eq.(
119
) do satisfy such conditions. It is noticed that either
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
or
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
are not all anti-commuting.

IV.8
Equivalent description of Dirac-
\And
Majorana-
\And
Weyl-type fermions as qubit-spinor fields with the same
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges in corresponding to complex
\And
self-conjugated
\And
chiral representations

It is shown that a self-conjugated qubit-spinor field can be described by a complex qubit-spinor field with a reduced qubit state. Similarly, a self-conjugated qubit-spinor field should be characterized equivalently by a chiral qubit-spinor field. In general, self-conjugated qubit-spinor field and complex qubit-spinor field can be regarded as Majorana-type fermion and Dirac-type fermion, respectively, and chiral qubit-spinor field is viewed as Weyl-type fermion.

Let us rewrite self-conjugated biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
into coherent states of two parts,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{2}}(x)+\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x),\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{2}}(x)\equiv\gamma_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x),\quad,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{3}}(x)+\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x),\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{3}}(x)=\Gamma_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

\displaystyle\gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\gamma_{5}),\quad\Gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\gamma_{7}),

(121)

with
\gamma_{5}
and
\gamma_{7}
given in Eq.(
IV.5
). Where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{3}}(x)
are defined as Weyl-type chiral spinor fermions in four- and six-dimensional spacetimes, which may be referred to as
chiral biqubit-spinor field
and
chiral triqubit-spinor field
, respectively. Actually, the two parts,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)
, or
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{3}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)
, should not be independent ones due to self-conjugate conditions. They are correlated via the following charge conjugation operation:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{2}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{c}(x),\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)+\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{c}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}^{c}(x),\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)+\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}^{c}(x).

(122)

From Eqs.(
IV.5
) and (
IV.8
), we arrive at the following general relations for chiral and self-conjugated as well as complex qubit-spinor fields:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)=\gamma_{-}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}(x)=\binom{0}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}(x)}=\gamma_{-}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}-}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)=\Gamma_{-}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\binom{0}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}(x)},

(123)

which provide explicit relations among Weyl-type and Majorana-type as well as Dirac-type fermions. In fact,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)
defines the usual Weyl fermion in four dimensional spacetime,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)\equiv\psi_{W}(x).

In terms of the chiral qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)
, the actions given in Eqs.(
85
) and (
87
) for self-conjugated biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields can equivalently be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{[\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)\tilde{\gamma}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{c}(x)+H.c.]

(124)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{p}(x)\partial_{\nu}\phi^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{Q}(\phi_{p}(x)\phi^{p}(x))^{2}\},

where the chiral biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)
with its charge-conjugated one
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{c}(x)
are defined as:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{T}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{\ast}(x),

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=\int d^{6}x\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)+H.c.],

(125)

for the chiral triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}(x)
.

From the above analyses, we arrive at the following equivalences for various formalisms of the actions:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{1}}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{M}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{W},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{3}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{D}.

(126)

which indicates that all qubit-spinor fields with complex and self-conjugated as well as chiral spinor representations can bring on equivalent actions as long as they contain the same independent degrees of freedom with equal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges.

Therefore, when Dirac-type and Majorana-type as well as Weyl-type fermions as local coherent states of qubits have the same independent degrees of freedom in correspondence to complex and self-conjugated as well as chiral spinor representations with equal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges, their hermitian actions are actually equivalent in Minkowski spacetime.

V
Local coherent state of 4-qubit as hyperqubit-spinor field with hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9)
\cong
SL(2,
\mathbb{O}
) in 10D Minkowski hyper-spacetime and the periodic feature of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge

Majorana fermion and Dirac fermion are shown to be described by biqubit-spinor field and triqubit-spinor field with respective to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}=2
and
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}=3
. In this section, we will discuss a local coherent state of four qubits (
Q_{N}
=4) based on the maximum coherence motion principle and local coherent-qubits motion postulate. Such a qubit-spinor field is referred to as
tetraqubit-spinor field
which spans 16-dimensional Hilbert space. Its maximally correlated motion leads to
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{c}}=D_{h}=10
, which determines ten-dimensional Minkowski spacetime with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,9). In general, we are going to refer to high dimensional spacetime as
hyper-spacetime
and its
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry as
hyperspin symmetry
. Also a qubit-spinor field formed with high qubit number (
Q_{N}\geq 4
) is referred to as
hyperqubit-spinor field
. The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of tetraqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field is found to be zero
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0
, which is no longer the same as qubit number
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}\neq Q_{N}
and unlike the cases with qubit number
Q_{N}\leq 3
. Instead, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of tetraqubit-spinor field turns out to the same as that of single-component spinor field. Actually, the single-component spinor field can be regarded as self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor field which may be referred to as
zeroqubit-spinor field
or
bit-spinor field
. Such an intriguing feature displays a periodic behavior of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge for tetraqubit-spinor field and zeroqubit-spinor field, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}-4=q_{c}=0
for the case of four qubits and
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}=q_{c}=0
for the case of zero qubit.

V.1
Local coherent state of 4-qubit in 16D Hilbert space with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
=0 as tetraqubit-spinor field in 10D Minkowski hyper-spacetime

Let us now consider a local coherent state of four qubits, which is constructed with 4-product qubit-basis states as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{4}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{4}},

(127)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{4}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{4}},

where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
are referred to as
tetraqubit-spinor field
and
self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
, respectively.
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{4}}(x)
present local distribution amplitudes with
16
independent degrees of freedoms, which characterize the local coherent state of four qubits (
Q_{N}=4
).

Based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle, we arrive at the hermitian action of tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{10}x\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x),

(128)

where
i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\equiv i\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mathsf{M}}}
is the linear self-adjoint derivative operator and
\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\;\mathsf{M}}
denotes the Kronecker symbol, with the vector indices
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,9
. The
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
form a vector in the qubit-spinor representation. The Latin alphabet
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\ldots
and the Latin alphabet starting from
\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}
are adopted to distinguish vector indices in non-coordinate spacetime and coordinate spacetime, respectively. All the Latin indices are raised and lowered by the constant metric matrices, i.e.,
\eta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
or
\eta_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=diag.(1,-1,\ldots,-1)
, and
\eta^{\mathsf{M}\mathsf{N}}
or
\eta_{\mathsf{M}\mathsf{N}}=diag.(1,-1,\ldots,-1)
.

The maximally correlated motion leads to the following ten real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
with
\mathsf{A}=0,1,\cdots,9
,

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}),\quad\bar{A}=1,\cdots,8,

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{1}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{3}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{4}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{5}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{6}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{7}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{8}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{9}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3},

(129)

which satisfy Clifford algebra relations,

\displaystyle\{\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\Upsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,9,

(130)

with
\Upsilon^{0}
the
16\times 16
unit matrix, i.e.,
\Upsilon^{0}\equiv I_{16}
.

The number of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
brings on
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{c}}=D_{h}=10
and determines
ten-dimensional Minkowski spacetime
. As there exists no antisymmetric normalized
\tilde{\Upsilon}
-matrix that anticommutes with real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
is zero, which is no longer to be equal to the qubit number. Instead, it is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=0.

In light of self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
, the action can be written as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{10}x\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x),

(131)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
. The
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}
get the following forms:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\Gamma^{0},\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\Gamma^{10}),\quad\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{7}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{8}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{9}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{10}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\equiv I_{16},

(132)

with
\Gamma^{10}
the
16\times 16
unit matrix, i.e.,
\Gamma^{10}\equiv I_{16}
. The self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{3}}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{3}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{2}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{2}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}-}^{2}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}+}^{2}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{3}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{3}}^{\ast},

(133)

which satisfies the following self-conjugate condition:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(134)

In general, we are going to refer to high dimensional Minkowski spacetime (
D_{h}\geq 10
) as
Minkowski hyper-spacetime
. Meanwhile, the tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
or self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
in 16-dimensional Hilbert space is referred to as
hyperqubit-spinor field
.

V.2
Hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9)
\cong
SL(2,
\mathbb{O}
) and intrinsic discrete symmetries of hyperqubit-spinor field in 10D Minkowski hyper-spacetime

It can be verified that the actions presented in Eq.(
128
) and (
131
) possess the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,9}\ltimes SO(1,9)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,9)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}=0)

(135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,9)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,9)\rtimes SG(1),

where SP(1,9) is
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry which is referred to as
hyperspin symmetry
. SP(
q_{c}
=0) denotes in formal
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
q_{c}=0
, which indicates that there exists no antisymmetric
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix and scalar coupling term. The semidirect product group PO(1,9) = P
{}^{1,9}\ltimes
SO(1,9) is Poincaré-type group symmetry/inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry with
P^{1,9}
denoting translation group symmetry and SO(1,9) representing Lorentz-type group symmetry in ten-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime of coordinates. SC(1)
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
SG(1) labels an associated symmetry with coincidental global scaling transformations of coordinates and fields. In fact, the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
” is used to express an associated symmetry in which the transformations of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) and global scaling symmetry SG(1) in Hilbert space should be coincidental to those of Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,9) and conformal scaling symmetry SC(1) in Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

The hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) of hyperqubit-spinor field and Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,9) of Minkowski hyper-spacetime as well as global scaling symmetry should obey the following transformation properties:

\displaystyle\Psi(x)\to\Psi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=S(\Lambda)\Psi(x),\quad S(\Lambda)\equiv e^{i\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\varpi_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}},

\displaystyle x^{\mathsf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathsf{M}}=L^{\mathsf{M}}_{\;\mathsf{N}}\,x^{\mathsf{N}},\quad S^{-1}(\Lambda)\Upsilon^{\mathsf{A}}S(\Lambda)=L^{\mathsf{A}}_{\;\mathsf{B}}\,\Upsilon^{\mathsf{B}},

\displaystyle S(\Lambda)\in SP(1,9),\quad L^{\mathsf{A}}_{\;\mathsf{B}}=L^{\mathsf{M}}_{\;\mathsf{N}}\in SO(1,9)\cong SP(1,9),

\displaystyle\Psi(x)\to\Psi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=e^{9\varpi/2}\Psi(x),\quad x^{\mu}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}=e^{-\varpi}x^{\mu},

\displaystyle e^{-\varpi}\in SC(1),\quad e^{9\varpi/2}\in SG(1),

(136)

with
\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
the group generators of SP(1,9) defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\equiv(\Sigma^{\bar{A}\bar{B}},\Sigma^{0\bar{A}},\Sigma^{09},\Sigma^{\bar{A}9}),\quad\Sigma^{\bar{A}\bar{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{\bar{B}}],

\displaystyle\Sigma^{0\bar{A}}=-\Sigma^{\bar{A}0}=\frac{i}{2}\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\quad\Sigma^{09}=-\Sigma^{90}=\frac{i}{2}\Upsilon^{9},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\bar{A}9}=-\Sigma^{9\bar{A}}=\frac{i}{2}\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}\Upsilon^{9},\quad\bar{A},\bar{B}=1,\cdots,8,

(137)

which operates on the tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field. Alternatively, the group generators
\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
get the following forms:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=(\Sigma^{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}},\Sigma^{0\tilde{A}},\Sigma^{0\,10},\Sigma^{\tilde{A}\,10}),\quad\Sigma^{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\Gamma^{\tilde{B}}],

\displaystyle\Sigma^{0\tilde{A}}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{0},\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}]=\frac{i}{2}\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\quad\Sigma^{0\,10}=-\Sigma^{10\,0}=\frac{i}{2}\Gamma^{0},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\tilde{A}\,10}=-\Sigma^{10\,\tilde{A}}=-\frac{i}{2}\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\quad\tilde{A},\tilde{B}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9,

(138)

which acts on the self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field.

As the
\Gamma
-matrix
\Gamma^{10}
is a unit matrix which is commuting with all others, not all group generators can be expressed as the commutative relations of
\Gamma
-matrices. The group generators of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) are in analogous to those of conformal group of spin symmetry SP(8)
\cong
SO(8), which is formally isomorphic to a special linear group of degree 2 over the
octonion

\mathbb{O}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle SP(1,9)\cong SL(2,\mathbb{O})\cong SO(1,9).

(139)

To ensure the invariance of the action in Eq.(
131
) under operations of discrete symmetries:
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{T}
, the self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
should transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}\equiv\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}\,T},\quad C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=i\Gamma^{0}\Gamma^{2}\Gamma^{6}\Gamma^{8}\Gamma^{9}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

for the charge conjugation, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\mathcal{P}^{-1}\equiv\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{p}(x)=P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x^{0},-x^{k},x^{10}),

\displaystyle P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}\,\dagger},\quad P_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\Gamma^{0}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3},

for the parity-inversion, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\mathcal{T}^{-1}\equiv\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{t}(x)=T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(-x^{0},x^{k},-x^{10}),

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{-1}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}\,T},\quad T_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\Gamma^{1}\Gamma^{3}\Gamma^{5}\Gamma^{7}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

for the time-reversal.

It is noticed that the spatial coordinate
x^{10}
in the self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor representation gets an unusual transformation property under both the parity-inversion and time-reversal operations as it undergoes a flip in sign for keeping the action to be invariant. When considering the combined operator
\vartheta\equiv\mathcal{PT}
, we arrive at a proper and conventional transformation as follows:

\displaystyle\vartheta\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\vartheta^{-1}=\theta\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(-x),\quad\theta^{-1}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}\theta=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}\,\ast},

which indicates that the combined operator
\vartheta
should be more appropriate for the self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
in 10-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

For the combined joint operation
\varTheta\equiv\mathcal{CPT}
acting on the self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
in 10-dimensional Hyper-spacetime, we obtain the following conventional transformation property:

\displaystyle\varTheta\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)\varTheta^{-1}=\Theta\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}^{T}(-x),

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}\Theta=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}\,\dagger},\quad\Theta\Gamma^{0}=\Gamma^{0}\Theta.

V.3
Self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor field in 2D Minkowski spacetime as zeroqubit-spinor field or bit-spinor field with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}=0

It is manifest that for a real spinor field with only one-component entity, there exists no scalar coupling term due to anti-commuting property of spinor field, so that it has a zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0
. Such a one-component entity real spinor field is regarded as
bit-spinor field
or
zeroqubit-spinor field
as it concerns zero qubit number
Q_{N}=0
, which can also be resulted by imposing a qubit-reduction condition on uniqubit-spinor field.

The uniqubit-spinor field with two independent degrees of freedom is shown to have a maximally correlated motion in three-dimensional spacetime. Let us define the
qubit-reduction operation
which reduces the independent degrees of freedom of qubit-spinor field into a half and results in a reduced qubit-spinor field. The
qubit-reduction condition
for uniqubit-spinor field is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x)\equiv\gamma_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x),\quad\gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\hat{\gamma}_{3}),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{3}=-i\gamma^{2}.

(140)

Explicitly, we obtain the following reduced qubit-spinor field:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}\pm}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\sigma_{3})\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x);\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}\pm}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\sigma_{1})\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}+}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)}{0},\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}-}(x)=\binom{0}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}+}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)},\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}-}(x)=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)}{-\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)},

which brings on
qubit-reduction spinor field
. Such a qubit-reduction spinor field has only one real component
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x)
or
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x)
, which may be referred to as
zeroqubit-spinor field
or
bit-spinor field
. Namely, each real component of uniqubit-spinor field is identified to be a zeroqubit-spinor field or a bit-spinor field in one-dimensional Hilbert space with zero qubit number
Q_{N}=0
.

The action for zeroqubit-spinor field can be deduced from Eq.(
44
) or Eq.(
40
) with the replacement of
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}}(x)
by
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x)
. Explicitly, we arrive at the following action of zeroqubit-spinor field:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x)

(141)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}\pm}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}\pm}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)i(\partial_{0}\pm\partial_{1})\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x),

with
a,\mu=0,1
. The action in Eq.(
141
) involves no scalar field coupling to the zeroqubit-spinor field, so that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge is just equal to the qubit number,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=0.

(142)

The action in Eq.(
141
) possesses the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,1}\ltimes SO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\times SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}=0)

(143)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\times SG(1),

where
SP(q_{c})=SP(0)
is in formal presented to indicate that there is no
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry and no scalar coupling for zeroqubit-spinor field, which is just applied to categorize both qubit-spinor field and spacetime dimension.

The qubit-reduction condition actually defines a chiral-like property of qubit-spinor field. It can be verified that the zeroqubit-spinor field as reduced uniqubit-spinor field satisfies the following
self-conjugated chiral condition
in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x)\mathcal{C}^{-1}=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}^{c}(x)=C_{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}^{T}(x)=C_{2}\gamma^{0}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}^{\ast}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}\pm}(x),

\displaystyle C_{2}^{-1}\gamma^{a}C_{2}=-\gamma^{a\,T},\;(a=0,1,2),

\displaystyle C_{2}=\begin{cases}\gamma^{0}=\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}\pm},\\
\sigma_{1}\gamma^{0}=-i\sigma_{2},&\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}\pm},\end{cases}

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}\pm}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}\pm}(x)
are referred to as
self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor fields
. To be specific, we may take the following notations:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}-}(x)\equiv(\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}-}(x),\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}-}(x)),\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{T}}+}(x)\equiv(\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{1}+}(x),\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}+}(x)),

(144)

to define self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor fields.

With the introduction of new variables for coordinates,

\displaystyle x^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(x^{0}\pm x^{1}),\quad\partial_{0}\pm\partial_{1}\equiv\partial_{\pm}\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\pm}},

the above action can simply be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)i\partial_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x).

(145)

Such an action is actually the same as the one given in Eq.(
6
) which is resulted directly from the simplest motion postulate and quadratic free motion postulate.

It can be checked that the action of zeroqubit-spinor field possesses both homogeneous and non-homogeneous scaling symmetries as well as translation group symmetry,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x^{\prime})=e^{\varpi/2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x),\quad x^{\mu}\to x^{\prime\mu}=e^{-\varpi}x^{\mu},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x^{\prime})=e^{\varpi_{\pm}/2}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x),\quad x^{\pm}\to x^{\prime\pm}=e^{-\varpi_{\pm}}x^{\pm},

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{\mu}+a^{\mu},\quad\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)\to\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x+a),

\displaystyle e^{-\varpi},\,e^{\varpi/2}\in SC(1)\cong SG(1),\quad e^{\varpi_{\pm}/2},e^{-\varpi_{\pm}}\in SP(1,1)\cong SO(1,1).

(146)

As there exists no scalar coupling to zeroqubit-spinor field, each zeroqubit-spinor field in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime keeps a free motion. The equation of motion can easily be read off as follows:

\displaystyle i\partial_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)=0,

(147)

its general solution is found to be:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x^{\mp})=\int\frac{dp}{\sqrt{2\pi}}[b_{\pm}(p)e^{-ipx^{\mp}}+b^{\dagger}_{\pm}(p)e^{ipx^{\mp}}],

(148)

with the momentum
p_{\pm}=p_{0}\pm p_{1}
and definitions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x^{-}):\quad p_{-}=0,\quad p_{0}=p_{1}=p,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x^{+}):\quad p_{+}=0,\quad p_{0}=-p_{1}=p.

Where
b_{\pm}(p)
and
b^{\dagger}_{\pm}(p)
are annihilation and creation operators which satisfy the following anti-commuting relation:

\displaystyle\{b_{\pm}(p),b^{\dagger}_{\pm}(p^{\prime})\}=\delta(p-p^{\prime}).

(149)

In general, the zeroqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{-}}(x^{+})
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{+}}(x^{-})
can be regarded as left-handed and right-handed freely moving
self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{L}}^{1}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{R}}^{1}}
, respectively.

For a finite spatial dimension with proper boundary condition, the zeroqubit-spinor field or bit-spinor field may be viewed as
zeroqubit-spinor string
or
bit-spinor string
in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

V.4
Periodic behavior of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge for qubit-spinor fields with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
=0,1,2,3,0 in corresponding to Hilbert space with dimensions
D_{H}
=1,2,4,8,16 and Minkowski spacetime with dimensions
D_{h}
=2,3,4,6,10

By applying for the orthonormal qubit basis states, we have analyzed four types of local coherent states of qubits. They contain the basic qubit-basis states, 2-product qubit-basis states, 3-product qubit-basis states and 4-product qubit-basis states, which bring about the uniqubit-spinor, biqubit-spinor, triqubit-spinor and tetraqubit-spinor fields, respectively. The independent degrees of freedom of qubit basis states are determined by qubit number
Q_{N}
, which span
2^{Q_{N}}
-dimensional Hilbert space and bring on
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges of qubit-spinor field based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle. The
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge determines the dimension of Minkowski spacetime and describes
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry in association with Poincaré-type group symmetry (also called inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry). The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge characterizes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry in correlation to scalar couplings and meanwhile reflects a periodic behavior.

The main properties of qubit-spinor fields and spacetime dimensions for
Q_{N}=0,1,2,3,4
are summarized in Table 1. It is seen that there exists a simple relation between
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
and qubit number
Q_{N}
for
Q_{N}\leq 3
. For the case
Q_{N}=4
, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge exhibits a periodic behavior with respect to the qubit number. To be specific, we can write down the simple relations as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=0,1,2,3,\qquad Q_{N}\leq 3,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=0,\quad\qquad Q_{N}=4,

(150)

which indicates that
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
should characterize
four categories of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
. We should consider the first five qubit numbers
Q_{N}=0,1,2,3,4
as
basic qubit numbers
, where
Q_{N}=0
is actually a trivial one.
