The dimension (
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}
) of Hilbert space for qubit-spinor field is determined by its local coherent state of qubits with a given qubit number
Q_{N}
, namely,
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}}
. The dimension (
D_{h}
) of Minkowski spacetime is determined from local coherent state of qubits via its maximally correlated motion with transverse dimension given by
D_{h}-2
. The local coherent state of qubits with qubit number
Q_{N}=4
has zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge, which is the same as the one of zeroqubit-spinor field or bit-spinor field and displays a periodic behavior of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge. Similarly, the spacetime dimensions
D_{h}=2,3,4,6,10
with respective to the basic qubit numbers
Q_{N}=0,1,2,3,4
are regarded as
basic spacetime dimensions
. In general, there exist four categories with respective to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
, which enables us to make a categorization for all hyperqubit-spinor fields and hyper-spacetime dimensions.

VI
Categorization of hyperqubit-spinor fields and hyper-spacetimes with periodic
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges and the genesis of hyper-spacetime dimension from categoric qubit number

The local coherent states of qubits built from 2-product and 3-product qubit-basis states are shown to characterize Majorana and Dirac fermions as biqubit-spinor and triqubit-spinor fields with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries SP(2)
\cong
U(1) and SP(3)
\cong
SU(2), respectively. The local coherent state of qubits formed from 4-product qubit-basis states brings on the tetraqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in 16-dimensional real Hilbert space and 10-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime, which is found to have a zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=0
that is the same as the one of zeroqubit-spinor field in two-dimensional spacetime. By further analyzing local coherent states of qubits constructed from higher product qubit-basis states in this section, we are able to deduce a definite periodic property of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
for arbitrary qubit number
Q_{N}
. In general, we are going to show that any hyperqubit-spinor field in Hilbert space and any Minkowski hyper-spacetime dimension determined from the maximally correlated free motion can be categorized into four classes in correspondence to four categories of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
, which can be carried out from classifying arbitrary qubit number
Q_{N}
into categoric qubit number denoted as
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period.

VI.1
Local coherent state of five qubits with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
=1 and the action of pentaqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in 11D hyper-spacetime

Let us begin with considering local coherent states of five qubits,
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
, based on the two types of 5-product qubit-basis states,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{5}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{5}},

(151)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{5}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{5}},

which are regarded as hyperqubit-spinor fields with
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}}=32
local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{5}}(x)
that span 32-dimensional Hilbert space. For convenience, we may refer to
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
as
pentaqubit-spinor field
and
self-conjugated pentaqubit-spinor field
, respectively.

Based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle, the action of pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field is constructed to be:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{11}x\{\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi(x)\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\varUpsilon}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)\},

(152)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,10
. Where eleven
32\times 32
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) and single
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix are given by the following forms:

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\quad\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},\quad\varUpsilon^{10}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

(153)

where
\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}
are
16\times 16
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices defined in Eq.(
V.1
).
\tilde{\varUpsilon}
is an imaginary antisymmetric normalized matrix which anticommutes with real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices except
\varUpsilon^{0}
.

It can be verified that
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varUpsilon}
satisfy the following Clifford algebra relations:

\displaystyle\{\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=2\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\varUpsilon},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=0,\quad\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,10,

(154)

which indicates that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x)
is given by the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=1.

(155)

Such a
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge reproduces the same one as that of uniqubit-spinor field in three-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. In structure, the three real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10}
and the single antisymmetric
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix
\tilde{\varUpsilon}
are analogous to those for uniqubit-spinor field presented in Eqs.(
41
) and (
42
). The remaining eight real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}}
(
\bar{A}=1,\cdots,8
) are correlated to extra eight spatial dimensions.

The action for self-conjugated pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field is constructed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{11}x\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\},

(156)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,11
. The
32\times 32

\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{11})
(
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) get the following structures:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{0},\quad\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}=\;\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{10}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\Gamma^{10},\quad\varGamma^{11}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\Gamma^{10},

(157)

with
16\times 16
matrices (
\Gamma^{0}
,
\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}
,
\Gamma^{10}
) given in Eq.(
V.1
). Obviously, three-dimensional spacetime in association with
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{0}
,
\Gamma^{10}
and
\Gamma^{11}
is formally in correspondence to that for self-conjugated uniqubit-spinor field shown in Eq.(
III.2
). The other eight
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}
(
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) characterize the maximally correlated free motion in extra eight spatial dimensions.

The self-conjugated pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field gets the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{2}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}^{\ast},

(158)

which satisfies the following self-conjugate condition:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(159)

It can be verified that the actions given in Eqs.(
152
) and (
156
) possess the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,10}\ltimes SO(1,10)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,10)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}=1)

(160)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,10)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,10)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(1),

where SP(1,10) represents maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry as hyperspin symmetry and SP(1)
\cong
O(1) denotes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=1
. PO(1,10) = P
{}^{1,10}\ltimes
SO(1,10) is Poincaré-type/inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry with translation group symmetry P
1,10
in 11-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime of coordinates. SC(1)
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
SG(1) labels an associated symmetry for global scaling transformations.

VI.2
Local coherent state of six qubits with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
=2 and the action of hexaqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in 12D hyper-spacetime

We now examine
local coherent state of six qubits
as hyperqubit-spinor field which is formed from 6-product qubit-basis states. The explicit forms are constructed as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{6}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{6}},

(161)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{6}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{6}},

where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)
is referred to as
hexaqubit-spinor field
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)
as
self-conjugated hexaqubit-spinor field
with qubit number
Q_{N}=6
. The local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{6}}(x)
contain
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}}=64
independent degrees of freedom, which spans a 64-dimensional Hilbert space.

Again based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle, the action of hexaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field is found to be:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{12}x\{\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)\},

(162)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,11
. The
64\times 64
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
with the notation
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10},\varUpsilon^{11})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) can be expressed into the following general form:

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\quad\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},\quad\varUpsilon^{10}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{11}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\quad\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{2}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

(163)

with
\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}
the
16\times 16
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
V.1
).
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}
(
p=1,2
) are two imaginary antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices which anticommute with real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices except the unit matrix
\varUpsilon^{0}
.

The matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}
satisfy the following Clifford algebra relations:

\displaystyle\{\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=2\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{q}\}=2\delta^{pq},

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=0,\quad\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,11,\quad p,q=1,2,

(164)

which implies that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of hexaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}}(x)
has the following relation with qubit number
Q_{N}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=2,

(165)

which is equal to the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of biqubit-spinor field in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

Actually, four real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices (
\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10},\varUpsilon^{11}
) and two imaginary antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices (
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{2}
) are analogous to those for biqubit-spinor field as shown in Eqs.(
IV.2
) and (
68
). The remaining eight real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}}
(
\bar{A}=1,\cdots,8
) characterize extra eight spatial dimensions.

In an analogous way, the action of self-conjugated hexaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field can be written as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{12}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)\},

(166)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,12
. The
64\times 64

\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{11},\varGamma^{12})
(
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) and
\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}
(
p=1,2
) can be constructed as follows:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{0},\quad\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{10}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Gamma^{10},\quad\varGamma^{11}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{12}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varGamma}^{1}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},\quad\tilde{\varGamma}^{2}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

(167)

with (
\Gamma^{0},\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\Gamma^{10}
) presented in Eq.(
V.1
). Where four
\varGamma
-matrices (
\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{9},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{11}
) and two
\tilde{\varGamma}
-matrices (
\tilde{\varGamma}^{1},\tilde{\varGamma}^{2}
) are similar to those for self-conjugated biqubit-spinor field in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime as shown in Eq.(
IV.5
).

The self-conjugated hexaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field has the following spinor structuce:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{5}}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{5}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{3}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{5}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{5}}^{\ast},

(168)

which satisfies the self-conjugate condition,

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(169)

The actions presented in Eq.(
162
) and (
166
) get the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,11}\ltimes SO(1,11)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,11)\times SG(1)\times SP(2)

(170)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,11)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,11)\times SG(1)\times SP(2),

where SP(1,11) denotes maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry as hyperspin symmetry. SP(2)
\cong
U(1) is the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry with respect to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=2
. PO(1,11) = P
{}^{1,11}\times
SO(1,11) represents Poincaré-type/inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry with SO(1,11) the Lorentz-type group symmetry and P
1,11
the translation group symmetry in 12-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

VI.3
Local coherent state of seven qubits with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
=3 and the action of heptaqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in 14D hyper-spacetime

We now come to consider a local coherent state which is form by 7-product qubit-basis states with qubit number
Q_{N}=7
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{7}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{7}},

(171)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{7}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{7}},

we may refer to hyperqubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)
as
heptaqubit-spinor field
and
self-conjugated heptaqubit-spinor field
, respectively. They involve
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}}=128
local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{7}}(x)
which span 128-dimensional Hilbert space.

The maximally correlated motion of heptaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}(x)
brings on the following hermitian action in 14-dimensional Minkowski spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{14}x\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{7}}(x),

(172)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,13
. Where
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
are
128\times 128
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices, which can be expressed as follows with the notation
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10},\varUpsilon^{11},\varUpsilon^{12},\varUpsilon^{13})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
):

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\quad\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{A},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},\quad\varUpsilon^{10}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{11}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\quad\varUpsilon^{12}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{13}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

(173)

with
\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}
the
16\times 16
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
V.1
). The three imaginary antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}
(
p=1,2,3
) are found to be:

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{2}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{3}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

(174)

which are anti-commuting with real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices except two matrices
\varUpsilon^{0}
and
\varUpsilon^{13}
. The matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}
satisfy the following Clifford algebra relations:

\displaystyle\{\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=2\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{q}\}=2\delta^{pq},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,13,\quad p,q=1,2,3;\quad\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=0,\;\mathsf{A}\neq 0,13,

(175)

where six real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices (
\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{9},\varUpsilon^{10},\varUpsilon^{11},\varUpsilon^{12},\varUpsilon^{13}
) and three antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices (
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{2},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{3}

IV.2
) and (
72
). The other eight real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}

\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}
(

) correlate to extra eight spatial dimensions. The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of heptaqubit-spinor field is the same as that of triqubit-spinor field, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=3
.

The action of self-conjugated heptaqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)
is easily read:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{14}x\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x),

(176)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,14
. The fourteen
128\times 128

\Gamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{11},\varGamma^{12},\varGamma^{13},\varGamma^{14})
(
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) are given by the following forms:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{0},\quad\varGamma^{10}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Gamma^{10},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{11}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes I_{16},\quad\varGamma^{12}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{13}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},\quad\varGamma^{14}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\quad\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9,

(177)

with (
\Gamma^{0},\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\Gamma^{10}
) presented in Eq.(
V.1
). Where six
\varGamma
-matrices (
\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{11},\varGamma^{12},\varGamma^{13},\varGamma^{14}
) are analogous to those for self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field in six-dimensional Minkowski spacetime as shown in Eq.(
IV.5
).

The self-conjugated heptaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{4}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}^{\ast},

(178)

which satisfies the following self-conjugate condition:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{7}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(179)

The actions given in Eq.(
172
) and (
176
) turn out to have the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,13}\ltimes SO(1,13)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,13)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(3)

(180)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,13)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,13)\rtimes SG(1)\times SU(2),

where SP(1,13) is hyperspin symmetry and PO(1,13) = P
{}^{1,13}\ltimes
SO(1,13) is Poincaré-type/inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry in 14-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime. SP(3)
\cong
SU(2) represents
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry which has the following group generators:

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16},

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes I_{16}.

It is obvious that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge is given by,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-4=3.

(181)

VI.4
Local coherent state of eight qubits and the action of octoqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in 18D hyper-spacetime in analogous to tetraqubit-spinor field in 10D hyper-spacetime and zeroqubit-spinor field in 2D spacetime

Let us analyze local coherent states of eight qubits formed from two types of 8-product qubit-basis states. They are hyperqubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)
in real qubit-basis and complex conjugated qubit-basis, respectively, with the following definitions:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{8}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\zeta_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\,\zeta_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\zeta_{s_{8}},

(182)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{8}}\tilde{\psi}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{8}},

where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
is referred to as
octoqubit-spinor field
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)
as
self-conjugated octoqubit-spinor field
. They contain
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{8}=256
local distribution amplitudes
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{8}}(x)
which span 256-dimensional Hilbert space.

Based on the local coherent-qubits motion postulate with following along the maximum coherence motion principle, we obtain the following action for octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{18}x\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x),

(183)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,17
. Where the eighteen
2^{8}\times 2^{8}
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8},\varUpsilon^{9+8})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) have the following structures:

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}=\Upsilon^{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\;\;\varUpsilon^{9+8}=\Upsilon^{9}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}=\Upsilon^{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\quad\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8}=\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},\;\;\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8,

(184)

with
\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}
the
16\times 16
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices defined in Eq.(
V.1
). The matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
satisfy the following Clifford algebra:

\displaystyle\{\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=2\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,17.

(185)

As there exists no antisymmetric normalized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix which anticommutes with real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices, the octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field has zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge. Such a
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge is given by a relation with qubit number as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}-8=0,

(186)

which repeats the cases for tetraqubit-spinor field in ten-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime and zeroqubit-spinor field in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. Where the ten real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}},\varUpsilon^{9+8}
are similar to those of tetraqubit-spinor field in 10-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime as shown in Eq.(
V.1
), and the remaining eight real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8}
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) involve additional eight spatial dimensions. Alternatively, the two real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{9+8}
are similar to the ones for self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor field or zeroqubit-spinor field in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime as shown in Eq.(
41
), while the other sixteen real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}}
and
\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8}
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) concern extra sixteen spatial dimensions.

The action for self-conjugated octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{18}x\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x),

(187)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,18
. Where
2^{8}\times 2^{8}

\Gamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}},\varGamma^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+9},\varGamma^{10+8})
(
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
,
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
) have the following forms:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\Gamma^{0}\otimes\Gamma^{0},\quad\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}=\Gamma^{0}\otimes\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+9}=\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}\otimes\Gamma^{10},\quad\varGamma^{10+8}=\Gamma^{10}\otimes\Gamma^{10},

(188)

with
\Gamma^{0}
,
\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}
and
\Gamma^{10}
the
16\times 16

{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
V.1
).

It is noticed that the hyper-spacetime in association with
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{0}
,
\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}
and
\varGamma^{10+8}
describes a free motion in ten-dimensional spacetime out of eighteen-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime, which is in analogous to the case for self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field shown in Eq.(
128
). The remaining eight
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varGamma^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+9}
characterize maximally correlated motion in extra eight spatial dimensions. On the other hand, we may regard two-dimensional spacetime in association with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varGamma^{0}
and
\varGamma^{10+8}
as basic spacetime for describing a longitudinal free motion, which is in analogous to a free motion of self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor field or zeroqubit-spinor field shown in Eq.(
141
), whereas the other sixteen
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varGamma^{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}}
and
\varGamma^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+9}
reflect the maximally correlated transverse motion in sixteen spatial dimensions.

The self-conjugated octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)
as hyperqubit-spinor field is explicitly defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{7}}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{7}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{6}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{Q}^{6}_{\mathsf{C}-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{Q}^{6}_{\mathsf{C}+}}(x)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}--}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}-+}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}+-}}(x)\\
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}^{5}_{\mathsf{C}++}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{C}}^{7}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{7}}^{\ast},

(189)

which satisfies the following self-conjugate condition:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(190)

The maximal associated symmetry of the actions in Eq.(
183
) and (
187
) is found to be,

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,17}\ltimes SO(1,17)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,17)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}=0)

(191)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,17)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,17)\rtimes SG(1),

where SP(1,17) is maximal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry as hyperspin symmetry. The semidirect group PO(1,17) = P
{}^{1,17}\ltimes
SO(1,17) characterizes Poinceré-type/inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group symmetry with
P^{1,17}
denoting the translation group symmetry in 18-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime of coordinates. SP(
q_{c}=0
) denotes in formal
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry to classify hyperqubit-spinor field, which indicates that there exist a class of hyperqubit-spinor field with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge that gets no scalar coupling.

VI.5
Classifications of arbitrary qubit number and spacetime dimension with four categories of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge and the genesis of spacetime dimension from categoric qubit number

When looking back to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge with a periodic behavior
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3,0
in correspondence to basic qubit-spinor fields, i.e., zeroqubit-spinor, uniqubit-spinor, biqubit-spinor, triqubit-spinor and tetraqubit-spinor fields, with respective to basic qubit numbers, i.e.,
q_{c}=Q_{N}=0,1,2,3,4
, we observe that local coherent states of qubits with high qubit number
Q_{N}\geq 4
bring on a periodic
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge given by a simple relation with qubit number, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=Q_{N}-4=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
, which characterize other four kinds of qubit-spinor fields: tetraqubit-spinor, pentaqubit-spinor, hexaqubit-spinor and heptaqubit-spinor fields with respective to four qubit numbers
Q_{N}=4,5,6,7
.

To be manifest, let us summarize the main properties of hyperqubit-spinor fields and hyper-spacetime dimensions in table 2.

From the properties shown in table 1 and table 2 for basic qubit-spinor fields and hyperqubit-spinor fields, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge is resulted as the mod 4 qubit number, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3=Q_{N}(\mbox{mod 4}),

(192)

which enables us to deduce a general theorem for the classification of qubit number.

Qubit number classification theorem
: any qubit number
Q_{N}
used to form product qubit-basis states and build local coherent state of qubits can be classified into four categories as categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
, which is characterized by four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
and integer number
k=0,1,\cdots
.

As four categories are in correspondence to four basic qubit numbers, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=0,1,2,3
, we are able to express any qubit number
Q_{N}
in terms of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge with the following general relation:

\displaystyle Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\equiv q_{c}+4k,

\displaystyle q_{c}=0,1,2,3,\quad k=0,1,2,\cdots,

(193)

which indicates that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}
plays an essential role as qubit category characteristic number. The periodic feature of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
for any qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}=q_{c}+4k
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c},k)}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c})}=q_{c},

\displaystyle q_{c}=0,1,2,3,\quad k=0,1,2,\cdots,

(194)

where the integer number
k
denotes as
cycle number of period
for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c},k)}
is regarded as
categoric
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period and
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
is considered as
categoric qubit number
in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period.

Following along the qubit number classification theorem and local coherent-qubits motion postulate, we are able to further deduce a general theorem for the classification of spacetime dimension.

Spacetime dimension classification theorem
: any spacetime dimension determined by the maximally correlated motion of qubit-spinor field with arbitrary categoric qubit number can be classified into four categories as categoric spacetime dimension
D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}
, which is characterized by four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
and periodic number
k=0,1,\cdots
.

As the basic spacetime dimensions generating from maximally correlated free motions of basic qubit-spinor fields with respective to four basic qubit numbers
Q_{N}=0,1,2,3
are determined to be
D_{q_{c}}=2,3,4,6
which are characterized by four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=Q_{N}=0,1,2,3
, we can express any spacetime dimension
D_{h}
in terms of categoric basic dimension
D_{q_{c}}
with the following general relation:

\displaystyle D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}\equiv D_{q_{c}}+8k,\quad k=0,1,2,\cdots,

\displaystyle D_{q_{c}}=2,3,4,6,\quad q_{c}=0,1,2,3,

(195)

which is referred to as
category-
q_{c}
spacetime dimension in
k
-th period
.
D_{q_{c}}
represents
basic spacetime dimension in category-
q_{c}
, which is completely determined by
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}
with the following identity:

\displaystyle D_{q_{c}}=2+2^{q_{c}-1}\theta(q_{c}-1),

(196)

which brings on four basic spacetime dimensions
D_{q_{c}}=2,3,4,6
with respective to four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
q_{c}=0,1,2,3
. Where
\theta(z)
is the
\theta
-function with
\theta(z)=1
for
z\geq 0
and
\theta(z)=0
for
z<0
.

Therefore, we are able to obtain the following general relation between categoric spacetime dimension
D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}
and categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
,

\displaystyle D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}=2Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-2(q_{c}-1)+2^{q_{c}-1}\theta(q_{c}-1),

(197)

which implies how the genesis of spacetime dimension is correlated to categoric qubit number and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge.

VI.6
Categorization of hyperqubit-spinor field and hyper-spacetime with four categories of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge and the action of hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
with hyperspin symmetry

In analogous to qubit number classification theorem and spacetime dimension classification theorem, it is straightforward to deduce a general categorization theorem for qubit-spinor field.

Qubit-spinor categorization theorem
: any qubit-spinor field as local coherent state of qubits with arbitrary qubit number can be categorized into four categories as categoric qubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
, which is characterized by four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2,3
and periodic number
k=0,1,\cdots
.

To make the categorizations for both hyperqubit-spinor field and hyper-spacetime through four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges with respective to four basic qubit numbers and four basic spacetime dimensions, it is appropriate to build the action of hyperqubit-spinor field as local coherent state of qubits with categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}=q_{c}+4k
in
k
-th period. Such a hyperqubit-spinor field is denoted by
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
and referred to as
category-
q_{c}
hyperqubit-spinor field in
k
-th period
, which spans
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}
dimensional Hilbert space defined on
D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}
dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period.

The hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)
with arbitrary qubit number
Q_{N}=n
is defined by real
n
-product qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\varsigma_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}}\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varsigma_{s_{n}},

with
\psi_{s_{1}\cdots s_{n}}(x)
regarded as evolving local distribution amplitudes. Any hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)
can always be classified into four categories
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{n}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
with respective to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
q_{c}=0,1,2,3
in
k
-th period.

For any qubit number in
Category-0
, i.e.,
Q_{N}^{(0,k)}=n=4k
, the action for
Category-0 hyperqubit-spinor field

\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}}(x)
with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0
in
k
-th period can generally be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(0)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int[dx]\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}}(x),

(198)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,D_{h}^{(0,k)}-1
. The maximally correlated motion of Category-0 hyperqubit-spinor field leads to genesis of
Category-0 hyper-spacetime
with dimension
D_{h}^{(0,k)}=2+8k
. For the case
k=0
, it provides the basic action for zeroqubit-spinor field or bit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{\pm}}(x)
in two-dimensional spacetime as shown in Eq.(
141
). When
k\geq 1
, the
2^{4k}\times 2^{4k}
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
can generally be expressed as
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j},\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
;
j=0,1,\cdots,k-1
). For the cases
k=1,2
, the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices are presented in Eqs.(
V.1
) and (
VI.4
), respectively. In the general case, we can express the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices by the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8\times 0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle j=1,\cdots,k-2,

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

(199)

where the matrices
\Upsilon^{0},\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\Upsilon^{9}
are
16\times 16
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices given in Eq.(
V.1
). The subscript of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{A}_{(i)}
(
i=1,2\cdots,k
) labels the column order in the product tensor.

For any qubit number in
Category-1
, i.e.,
Q_{N}^{(1,k)}=1+4k
, the action for
Category-1 hyperqubit-spinor field

\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x)
with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=1
in
k
-th period is generally obtained as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(1)}}=\int[dx]\{\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}(x)\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\varUpsilon}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x)\},

(200)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,D_{h}^{(1,k)}-1
. The
Category-1 hyper-spacetime
gets dimensions
D_{h}^{(1,k)}=3+8k
(
k=0,1\cdots
) with three-dimensional spacetime as a basic one. In the simplest case with
k=0
, the action for uniqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{1}}(x)
in three-dimensional spacetime is presented in Eq.(
44
). The
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrix for the case
k=1
are given in Eq.(
VI.1
).

In the general case
k\geq 1
, the
2^{4k+1}\times 2^{4k+1}
real symmetric normalized
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
are expressed as
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j},\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)},\varUpsilon^{10+8(k-1)})
(
\bar{\mathsf{A}}=1,\cdots,8
;
j=0,1,\cdots,k-1
), which can be obtained by extending straightforwardly the case
k=1
to the general case
k\geq 1
with the following explicit structures:

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle j=0,1,\cdots,k-1,

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{10+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)}.

(201)

Similarly, the action for

\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x)
with categoric qubit number

in the

-th period has the following general form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(2)}}=\int[dx]\{\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x)\},

(202)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,D_{h}^{(2,k)}-1
. The dimension of

. The simplest case with

For the general case

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle j=0,1,\cdots,k-1,

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{10+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{11+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)}.

(203)

In analogous, the action for

in the

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(3)}}=\int[dx]\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}}(x),

(204)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,\cdots,D_{h}^{(3,k)}-1

For the general case

\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{\bar{\mathsf{A}}+8j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle j=0,1,\cdots,k-1,

\displaystyle\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{9}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{9}_{(k)},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

(205)

The three imaginary antisymmetric normalized

\displaystyle\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\Upsilon^{0}_{(k-j)}\cdots\otimes\Upsilon^{0}_{(k)},
