(206)

The matrices
\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p}
satisfy the following Clifford algebra relations:

\displaystyle\{\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\delta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=2\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{q}\}=2\delta^{pq},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=1,\cdots,D_{h}^{(3,k)}-1,\quad p,q=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\varUpsilon}^{p},\varUpsilon^{\mathsf{A}}\}=0,\quad\mathsf{A}\neq\varUpsilon^{0},\varUpsilon^{13+8(k-1)}.

(207)

In general, the action for category-
q_{c}
hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
in
k
-th period possesses the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}-1}\ltimes SO(1,D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}-1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}-1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c})

(208)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}-1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SO(1,D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}-1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}).

VII
String-like qubit-spinor field with free motion in two-dimensional spacetime and the self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field with full anti-commuting
\Gamma
-matrices

The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge as qubit category characteristic number enables us to categorize all hyperqubit-spinor fields by utilizing categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}=q_{c}+4k
in the
k
-th period (
k=0,1,\cdots
) and meanwhile to construct category-
q_{c}
hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
in hyper-spacetime based on the periodic feature of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c},k)}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c})}=q_{c}
. The dimension of hyper-spacetime is also classified into categoric spacetime dimension
D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}=D_{q_{c}}+8k
with
D_{q_{c}}
representing four basic spacetime dimensions
D_{q_{c}}=2,3,4,6
in correspondence to four
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
Q_{c}=0,1,2,3
. In this section, we are going to investigate self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor structure. On one hand, a self-conjugated chiral-like qubit-spinor structure allows us to construct string-like qubit-spinor field with free motion in two-dimensional spacetime from self-conjugated qubit-spinor field defined in
D_{h}
-dimensional spacetime. On the other hand, a self-conjugate qubit-spinor field with arbitrary qubit number
Q_{N}
in category-
q_{c}
can always be described by a self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field with one additional qubit number
Q_{N}+1
but with the same category-
q_{c}
in large Hilbert space. As a specific example, a massless Dirac fermion as category-3 self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field in six-dimensional spacetime is shown to be described by self-conjugated chiral tetraqubit-spinor field in category-3. In particular, we will demonstrate that in light of self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field, all
\Gamma
-matrices represented in large Hilbert space become anti-commuting.

VII.1
Self-conjugated chiral-like spinor structure and string-like qubit-spinor field

Based on the periodic feature of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c},k)}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c})}=q_{c}
, all qubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
in the same category-
q_{c}
but with different period (
k=1,2,\cdots
) should possess the same
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, and the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices should have a similar matrix structure in correlation to four basic spacetime dimensions
D_{q_{c}}=2,3,4,6
.

Let us begin with examining the zeroqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{0}}(x)
, tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
and octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
in category-0. As the zeroqubit-spinor field can be regarded as a self-conjugated chiral uniqubit-spinor field in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime shown in Eq.(
141
), the relevant two
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices are in analogous to two
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{0}
and
\Upsilon^{9}
for tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
or
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{0}
and
\Gamma^{10}
for self-conjugated tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
, such two
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices characterize longitudinal free motion in a relevant two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

To make an explicit illustration, it is useful to express the tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)
as the sum of two
self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor fields

\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)+\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)=\Upsilon_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}(x),\quad\Upsilon_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\Upsilon^{9}).

(209)

In a similar way, we make the following decomposition for
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)+\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)=\Gamma_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}(x),\quad\Gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\Gamma^{0}),

(210)

where the project operators
\Upsilon_{\pm}
and
\Gamma_{\pm}
define a self-conjugated chiral-like structure for tetraqubit-spinor field, which allows us to rewrite the actions in Eqs.(
128
) and (
131
) into the following forms in ten-dimensional hyper-spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{10}x\frac{1}{2}\{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x)+\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)

(211)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\bar{A}}^{\;\bar{M}}\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)+\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{\bar{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}i\partial_{\bar{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x)\},

with
a,\mu=0,9
and
\bar{A},\bar{M}=1,2,\cdots,8
, and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{10}x\frac{1}{2}\{\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)+\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)

(212)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)\delta_{\tilde{A}}^{\;\tilde{M}}\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}i\partial_{\tilde{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)+\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)\delta_{\tilde{A}}^{\;\tilde{M}}\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}i\partial_{\tilde{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)\},

with
a,\mu=0,10
and
\tilde{A},\tilde{M}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
.

It is noticed from the above actions that each self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x)
(
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)
) or
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)
(
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)
) gets a free motion only in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. The free motion in other eight spatial dimensions only occurs when two self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor fields with opposite chirality
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)
or
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)
present simultaneously and obey the maximum coherence motion principle. Namely, their interferential terms for the derivative operators in association with eight
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices
\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}
(
\bar{A}=1,2,\cdots,8
) or eight
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
), describe the maximally correlated motion in eight spatial dimensions. Therefore, the action for each self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor field can be expressed as follows in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Upsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x),

(213)

with
a,\mu=0,9
for
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)
and
a,\mu=0,10
for
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)
.

Similarly, it can be verified that the
self-conjugated chiral-like octoqubit-spinor fields

\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)
get a free motion in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime. Their actions can be presented as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\varUpsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\varGamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x),

(214)

with
a,\mu=0,17
for
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x)
and
a,\mu=0,18
for
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)
. Where we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x)=\varUpsilon_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x),\quad\varUpsilon_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\varUpsilon^{9+8}),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)=\varGamma_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x),\quad\varGamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\varGamma^{0}).

(215)

with
\varUpsilon^{9+8}\equiv\varUpsilon^{17}
given in Eq.(
VI.4
) and
\varGamma^{0}
presented in Eq.(
VI.4
). Where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}}(x)
are octoqubit-spinor field and self-conjugated octoqubit-spinor field defined in Eq.(
VI.4
), respectively, which are shown in Eqs.(
183
) and (
187
) to have a free motion in 18-dimensional hyper-spacetime.

It is easy to check that the above actions possess
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,1) and global scaling symmetry SG(1) in association with Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,1) and translation group symmetry
P^{1,1}
as well as conformal scaling symmetry SC(1). Meanwhile, the
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry concerning
D_{h}-2
spatial dimensions becomes an intrinsic hyperspin symmetry SP(
D_{h}
-2).

For the self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)
(
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)
), the associated symmetry is given by,

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,1}\ltimes SO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(8)

(216)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\times SG(1)\times SP(8),

where SP(8)
\cong
SO(8) provides a maximal internal hyperspin symmetry.

For the self-conjugated chiral-like octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x)
(
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)
), the associated symmetry reads as follows:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,1}\ltimes SO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(16)

(217)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(16),

which brings on the maximal internal hyperspin symmetry SP(16)
\cong
SO(16).

It is straightforward to generalize the above analyses to arbitrary hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0 with
self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor structure
. A self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0 can generally be defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}(x)=\varUpsilon_{\pm}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}}(x),\quad\varUpsilon_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\Upsilon^{9+8(k-1)}),

(218)

with
\varUpsilon_{\pm}
the project operators and
k
the periodic number (
k=0,1,\cdots
). For the cases with
k=0,1,2
, they are already analyzed above. For a general case
k\geq 3
, the action for self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}(x)
in category-0 gets the following general form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(0)}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\varUpsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}(x),

(219)

which has a free motion only in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime with
a,\mu=0,(1+8k)
. Such an action possesses the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,1}\ltimes SO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(8k)

(220)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(8k),

where SP(8k) represents internal hyperspin symmetry.

As a self-conjugated chiral-like qubit-spinor field has a free motion only in two-dimensional spacetime, we are naturally inspired to associate it with string theory
ST1
;
ST2
. So, the self-conjugated chiral-like tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)
or
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)
and self-conjugated chiral-like octoqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{8}\pm}(x)
or
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{8}\pm}(x)
may be regarded as a
string-like tetraqubit-spinor field
and
string-like octoqubit-spinor field
, respectively. In general, a self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}(x)
with a free motion in two-dimensional spacetime is viewed as a
string-like hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0
, which is in analogous to the zeroqubit-spinor field shown in Eq.(
141
).

The above discussions can be extended to any hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
with self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor structure, i.e.,
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}(x)
, its action can be written into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}^{\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\varUpsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}(x),

(221)

with
a,\mu=0,(1+8k)
for the
k
-th period of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge and the definition,

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}(x)=\varUpsilon_{\pm}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x),\quad\varUpsilon_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}).

(222)

To be more general, we can define a
string-like hyperqubit-spinor field
in category-
q_{c}
with any self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor structure, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}^{(j)}(x)=\varUpsilon_{\pm}^{j}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}^{(j)}(x),\quad\varUpsilon_{\pm}^{j}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\varUpsilon^{j}),

(223)

where
\varUpsilon^{j}
can be any
\varUpsilon
-matrix with
j=1,2,\cdots,1+8k
for the
k
-th period of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{(q_{c},k)}
. The action for such a general string-like hyperqubit-spinor field is presented as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}^{(j)\dagger}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\varUpsilon^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}^{(j)}(x),

(224)

with
a,\mu=0,j
.

The action for a general string-like hyperqubit-spinor field possesses the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,1}\ltimes SO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(D_{q_{c}}-2+8k)\times SP(q_{c})

(225)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(D_{q_{c}}-2+8k)\times SP(q_{c}),

with
D_{q_{c}}=3,4,6
for
q_{c}=1,2,3
. SP
(D_{q_{c}}-2+8k)\times
SP(
q_{c}
) represents the maximal internal symmetry for self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period.

Therefore, we can always construct a
self-conjugated chiral-like qubit-spinor field

\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}(x)
from a general qubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
with categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
, so that it moves freely only in two-dimensional spacetime. Such a self-conjugated chiral-like qubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}\pm}(x)
is referred to as
string-like qubit-spinor field
.

VII.2
Self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field and full anti-commuting
\Gamma
-matrices in an extended Hilbert space

When the dimension
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}
of Hilbert space spanned by product qubit basis states is larger than the dimension
D_{h}
of Minkowski spacetime determined from the maximally correlated motion of qubit-spinor field, the motion correlation
\varGamma
-matrices are not all satisfying anti-commuting relations, which indicates that such a Hilbert space formed by product qubit basis states is not large enough. To make all
\varGamma
-matrices becoming anti-commuting, it is appropriate to consider self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor structure for qubit-spinor field represented in an enlarged Hilbert space. A self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field can generally be constructed from self-conjugated qubit-spinor field with one additional qubit number in chiral qubit-spinor representation so as to keep the same independent degrees of freedom with equal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge.

Let us begin with examining the tetraqubit-spinor field. As shown in Eq.(
V.2
), not all
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}
defined in Eq.(
V.1
) are anti-commuting. The tetraqubit-spinor field in 16-dimensional Hilbert space belongs to a qubit-spinor representation in vector space of eight dimensions, i.e.,
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{D/2}=16
with
D=8
. Whereas its maximally correlated motion brings on ten-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime
D_{h}=10
. It naturally inspires us to represent such a tetraqubit-spinor field with 16 independent degrees of freedom in a qubit-spinor representation of vector space with the same dimension as that of Minkowski hyper-spacetime, i.e.,
D=D_{h}
.

It is straightforward to construct a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)
in terms of self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}\pm}(x)
defined in Eq.(
VII.1
), its explicit form is given as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}-}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}+}(x)}\equiv\varGamma_{-}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}),\quad\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{9},

(226)

where the 16 independent degrees of freedom of tetraqubit-spinor field are represented as a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field with chiral qubit-spinor representation defined in 10 dimensions
D=10
. The action in Eq.(
211
) can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}^{4}}=\int d^{10}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x),

(227)

with
\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=\Psi^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}\varGamma^{0}
. The
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{A}},\varGamma^{10})
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) are explicitly given as follows:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{2}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},\qquad\varGamma^{10}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\Upsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}},\quad\tilde{A}=1,2,3=\bar{A},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\Upsilon^{\bar{A}+1},\;\;\tilde{A}=5,\cdots,9=\bar{A}+1,

(228)

where
\Upsilon^{0}
and
\Upsilon^{\bar{A}}
(
\bar{A}=1,\cdots,8
) are presented in Eq.(
V.1
).

Similarly, the action in Eq.(
212
) can be expressed into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\int d^{10}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x),

(229)

with
\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)=\Psi^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}\varGamma^{0}
. The
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{10},\varGamma^{\tilde{A}})
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) get the following structure:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\Gamma^{0},\quad\varGamma^{10}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\Gamma^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\Gamma^{\tilde{A}},\quad\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9,

(230)

where
\Gamma^{0}
and
\Gamma^{\tilde{A}}
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
) are defined in Eq.(
V.1
).
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)
is prsented as a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field with the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}+}(x)}\equiv\varGamma_{-}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}),\quad\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\Gamma^{0},

(231)

where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}\pm}(x)
are the self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor fields defined in Eq.(
VII.1
).

It can be verified that both
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)
satisfy the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x),\quad\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x),\quad\gamma_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)=-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}=\varGamma^{0},\quad C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}=\sigma_{1}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(232)

with
C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}
defined in Eq.(
V.2
).

The self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}^{5}-}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}-}(x)
are referred to as
self-conjugated chiral pentaqubit-spinor fields
. In such a chiral qubit-spinor representation, all
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}
defined in Eqs.(
VII.2
) and (
VII.2
) become anti-commuting,

\displaystyle\{\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\eta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},

(233)

with
\eta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\mathop{\rm diag}.(1,-1,\cdots,-1)
. Thus the group generators of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) can simply be given by the commutators of
\varGamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}],

(234)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
.

The above analysis can be generalized to build the action for any hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
.
For the general hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
0
, the action can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(1)}-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(0)}}=\int d^{D_{h}^{(0,k)}}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}(x),

(235)

with
\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}(x)=\Psi^{\dagger}_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}\varGamma^{0}
. Where
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}(x)
defines a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field with the following form:

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}-}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}-}(x)}{\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}+}(x)}\equiv\varGamma_{-}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{D_{h}^{(0,k)}+1}),\quad\gamma_{D_{h}^{(0,k)}+1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)},

(236)

where
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(0,k)}\pm}(x)
are the so-called self-conjugated chiral-like hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0 defined in Eq.(
218
), and
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x)
is a hyperqubit-spinor field in category-1. The
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j},\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)})
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
;
j=1,\cdots,k-1
;
k=1,2,\cdots,
) have the following explicit structure:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{2}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}+8j},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

(237)

where
\varUpsilon^{0}
,
\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)}
and
\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}+8j}
(
\bar{A}=1,\cdots,8
) are presented in Eq.(
VI.6
).

Analogously, for the general hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
1
, its action can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(2)}-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(1)}}=\int d^{D_{h}^{(1,k)}}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)

(238)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)\tilde{\varGamma}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)\},

with
\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)=\Psi^{\dagger}_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}\varGamma^{0}
.
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)
is defined as a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field,

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}-}(x)=\binom{0}{\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(1,k)}}(x)}\equiv\varGamma_{-}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{D_{h}^{(1,k)}+1}),\quad\gamma_{D_{h}^{(1,k)}+1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

(239)

where
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x)
is a hyperqubit-spinor field in category-2. The
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j},\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)},\varGamma^{11+8(k-1)},\tilde{\varGamma})
(
\tilde{A}=1,2,3,5,\cdots,9
;
j=1,\cdots,k-1
;
k=1,\cdots,
) get the following structure:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}+8j},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{11+8(k-1)}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varGamma}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0}.

(240)

Similarly, we are able to rewrite the action for the general hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
2
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(3)}-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{N}^{(2)}}=\int d^{D_{h}^{(2,k)}}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)

(241)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{2}\tilde{\phi}_{p}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)\},

with
\bar{\Psi}_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)=\Psi^{\dagger}_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}\varGamma^{0}
.
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)
is defined as a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field,

\displaystyle\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}-}(x)=\binom{0}{\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(2,k)}}(x)}\equiv\varGamma_{-}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{D_{h}^{(2,k)}+1}),\quad\gamma_{D_{h}^{(2,k)}+1}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

(242)

where
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(3,k)}}(x)
is a hyperqubit-spinor field in category-3. The
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\varGamma^{0},\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j},\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)},\varGamma^{11+8(k-1)},\varGamma^{12+8(k-1)}
) (

;

;

) and

(

\displaystyle\varGamma^{0}=\;\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\tilde{A}+8j}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\varUpsilon^{\bar{A}+8j},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{10+8(k-1)}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\varUpsilon^{9+8(k-1)},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{11+8(k-1)}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{12+8(k-1)}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varGamma}^{1}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varGamma}^{2}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\varUpsilon^{0}.

(243)

It can be verified that
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)
(
q_{c}=1,2,3
) satisfies the following self-conjugated chiral condition:

\displaystyle\Psi^{c}_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)=C_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}\bar{\Psi}^{T}_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)=\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x),\quad C_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}=\varGamma^{0},

\displaystyle\gamma_{D_{h}^{(q_{c}-1,k)}+1}\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)=-\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)=\gamma_{D_{h}^{(q_{c}-1,k)}+1}\Psi^{c}_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x),

(244)

where
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}-}(x)
(
