) is referred to as self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field in category-(
q_{c}
-1) and
k
-th period. In such a self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor representation, all
\varGamma
-matrices become anti-commuting,

\displaystyle\{\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\eta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\quad\{\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}\}=0,\quad\{\tilde{\varGamma}^{p},\tilde{\varGamma}^{q}\}=2\eta^{pq},

(245)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,D_{h}^{(q_{c}-1,k)}
and
p,q=1,\cdots,q_{c}-1
. The group generators of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}^{(q_{c}-1,k)}
-1) and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(
q_{c}-1
) are directly defined by the commutators of
\varGamma
-matrices as follows:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}],\quad\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\varGamma}^{p},\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}].

(246)

Therefore, when the categoric hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
with categoric qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}
in category-
q_{c}
and
k
-th period is represented by a self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c}+1,k)}-}(x)
constructed from the hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c}+1,k)}}(x)
in category-(
q_{c}
+1) with one additional qubit number
Q_{N}^{(q_{c}+1,k)}=Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}+1
, all
\varGamma
-matrices that reflect both
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge of hyperspin symmetry and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry become full anti-commuting. The categoric hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}(x)
and self-conjugated chiral hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{Q_{N}^{(q_{c}+1,k)}-}(x)
are defined in Hilbert space with dimensions
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=2^{Q_{N}^{(q_{c},k)}+1}
, respectively, but they contain the same independent degrees of freedom with equal
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge.

VII.3
Massless Dirac fermion as self-conjugated chiral tetraqubit-spinor field in category-
3
with full anti-commuting
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-matrices and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices in 6D spacetime

The actions in Eqs.(
98
) and (
110
) indicate that a massless Dirac fermion can be described by a self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
given in Eq.(
IV.5
) or equivalently by a self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
presented in Eq.(
IV.7
) via a unitary transformation. Nevertheless, in such qubit-spinor representations, the
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
presented in Eq.(
IV.5
) or Eq.(
IV.7
) are not all anti-commuting, which motivates us to consider an extended qubit-spinor representation with self-conjugated chiral spinor structure. In fact, leptons and quarks in the electroweak interaction of SM behave as Weyl fermions with a chiral spinor structure in four-dimensional spacetime.

The self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
can generally be rewritten into the sum of two

self-conjugated chiral triqubit-spinor fields
as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)+\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x),

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x)=\hat{\Gamma}_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\hat{\gamma}_{7}),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{7}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5},

(247)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}-}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}-}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{2}}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}+(x)}}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}+}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)}\equiv\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{2}}(x)}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{2}}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}\pm}^{\;\bar{c}}\equiv C_{D}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}\pm}^{T},\quad C_{D}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(248)

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}-}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}+}(x)
are chiral complex biqubit-spinor fields,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}\pm}\equiv\gamma_{\pm}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{2}}\equiv\gamma_{\pm}\psi_{D},

\displaystyle\gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\gamma_{5}),\quad\gamma_{5}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(249)

which are regarded as the left-handed and right-handed Weyl fermions defined from Dirac fermion in the spinor representation of four dimensions,

In light of
self-conjugated chiral triqubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x)
which satisfy the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}^{T}(x)=\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{7}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x)=\hat{\gamma}_{7}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}^{c}(x)=\pm\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=-i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(250)

the action in Eq.(
87
) can be rewritten into the following form in six-dimensional spacetime:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)

(251)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)\delta_{k}^{\;m}\Gamma^{k}i\partial_{m}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)\delta_{k}^{\;m}\Gamma^{k}i\partial_{m}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)],

with
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
and
k,m=5,6
. Where the correlation motions in the fifth and sixth spatial dimensions can only appear between two self-conjugated chiral triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)
and
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)
.

By introducing an alternative qubit-spinor field
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}
with satisfying the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}=\binom{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}}{\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}},

\displaystyle\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}^{c}(x)=C_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}\bar{\psi}^{T}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)=\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{9}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}=\hat{\gamma}_{9}\psi^{c}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}=-\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-},\quad\hat{\gamma}_{9}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{7},

\displaystyle C_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}=-i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(252)

we can express the above action into the following simple form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\varGamma^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x),

(253)

with
\bar{\psi}_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)=\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}^{\dagger}(x)\varGamma^{0}
. We may refer to
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}-}(x)
as
self-conjugated chiral tetraqubit-spinor field
. In such a chiral spinor representation, the
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\hat{a}}\equiv(\varGamma^{a},\varGamma^{k})
(
a=0,1,2,3
;
k=5,6
) get following explicit form:

\displaystyle\varGamma^{a}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma^{a},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{5}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{6}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5},

(254)

with
\gamma^{a}
defined in Eq.(
IV.5
). The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}=\varGamma^{6+p}
(
p=1,2,3
) are found to be,

\displaystyle\varGamma^{7}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{1},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{8}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{2},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{9}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{3}.

(255)

It can be verified that all
\Gamma
-matrices become anti-commuting,

\displaystyle\{\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\Gamma^{\hat{b}}\}=2\eta^{\hat{a}\hat{b}},\quad\{\tilde{\Gamma}^{p},\tilde{\Gamma}^{q}\}=2\eta^{pq},\quad\{\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}\}=0.

(256)

The group generators of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,5) and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(3) can simply be given by the commutators of
\varGamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\hat{a}},\varGamma^{\hat{b}}],\quad\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq}=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\varGamma}^{p},\tilde{\varGamma}^{q}],

(257)

where
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(3)
\cong
SU(2) reflects an U(1) charge-spin between the chiral complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}\pm}(x)
and its charge conjugated one
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}\pm}^{\;\bar{c}}
as well as a rotational-spin property between two self-conjugated chiral triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)
and
\psi_{\hat{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)
.

VIII
Entangled hyperqubit-spinor field from locally entangled-qubits motion postulate and inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as basic symmetry of lepton-quark states

It is shown that in light of self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor structure, all
\Gamma
-matrices become anti-commuting and the group generators of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries are directly presented by the commutators of
\Gamma
-matrices, which provides an appropriate qubit-spinor representation in Hilbert space for building the action of qubit-spinor field. In this section, we are going to investigate further the intrinsic property of self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field, which allows us to extend the motion-correlation
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry to be an
inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry
in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry. In particular, we are led to make a
locally entangled-qubits motion postulate
by which a locally entangled state of qubits is proposed to be as an entangled qubit-spinor field, which enables us to show how inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field reflect basic symmetries of lepton-quark states.

VIII.1
Entangled state in quantum theory and locally entangled-qubits motion postulate

It is known in quantum physics that multiple qubits can exhibit quantum entanglement, which is regarded as the distinguishing feature between a qubit and a bit. For instance, the entanglement of quantum state of two qubits can be denoted either by Dirac “bra-ket” notation or by spin-like “column-row” notation as follows:

\displaystyle|\Psi_{E}\rangle=\alpha_{00}|0\rangle\otimes|0\rangle+\alpha_{11}|1\rangle\otimes|1\rangle\equiv\alpha_{00}|00\rangle+\alpha_{11}|11\rangle,

\displaystyle\Psi_{E}=\alpha_{++}\binom{1}{0}\otimes\binom{1}{0}+\alpha_{--}\binom{0}{1}\otimes\binom{0}{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{++}\\
0\\
0\\
\alpha_{--}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle|\alpha_{00}|^{2}+|\alpha_{11}|^{2}=1,\quad|\alpha_{++}|^{2}+|\alpha_{--}|^{2}=1,

(258)

where
|\alpha_{00}|^{2}
or
|\alpha_{++}|^{2}
and
|\alpha_{11}|^{2}
or
|\alpha_{--}|^{2}
are probabilities of measuring
|0\rangle\otimes|0\rangle
or
\binom{1}{0}\otimes\binom{1}{0}
and
|1\rangle\otimes|1\rangle
or
\binom{0}{1}\otimes\binom{0}{1}
, respectively. For
\alpha_{00}=\alpha_{11}=1/{\sqrt{2}}
or
\alpha_{++}=\alpha_{--}=1/{\sqrt{2}}
, it is called equal superposition with equal probability
|1/{\sqrt{2}}|^{2}=1/2
.

In general, an entangled system is considered to be the system in which its quantum state cannot be factored as a product of pure states.

For a more formal analysis, let us consider two noninteracting systems
S
and
S^{\prime}
with Hilbert spaces H
S
and H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
, respectively. The Hilbert space of their composite system is defined by the tensor product H
{}_{S}\otimes
H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
. If two systems are in states
\Psi_{S}
and
\Psi_{S^{\prime}}
, the state of the composite system is given by the tensor product
\Psi_{S}\otimes\Psi_{S^{\prime}}
. If states of the composite system can be represented in such a form in the tensor product, they are called product states or separable states. Whereas not all states can be expressed as product states or separable states.

Given a basis
\{\xi_{i}\}
for H
S
and a basis
\{\xi^{\prime}_{i}\}
for H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
, a general state in H
{}_{S}\otimes
H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi=\sum_{i,j}\psi_{ij}\,\xi_{i}\otimes\xi^{\prime}_{j},

where
\Psi
is called separable if

\displaystyle\psi_{ij}=\psi_{i}\,\psi^{\prime}_{j},\quad\Psi=\psi\otimes\psi^{\prime},

\displaystyle\psi=\sum_{i}\psi_{i}\,\xi_{i},\quad\psi^{\prime}=\sum_{j}\psi^{\prime}_{j}\,\xi^{\prime}_{j},

with
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
representing two vectors.

When
\Psi
becomes inseparable if

\displaystyle\psi_{ij}\neq\psi_{i}\,\psi^{\prime}_{j},\quad\Psi\neq\psi\otimes\psi^{\prime},

hold for all vectors
\psi_{i}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}
.

If a state is inseparable, it is referred to as
entangled state
. As an example, let us consider the simplest case with two basis states
\varsigma_{s}=\{|0\rangle,|1\rangle\}
or
\varsigma_{s}=\{\binom{1}{0},\binom{0}{1}\}

of Hilbert space H
S
and two basis states
\varsigma_{s^{\prime}}=\{|0\rangle,|1\rangle\}
or
\varsigma_{s^{\prime}}=\{\binom{1}{0},\binom{0}{1}\}
of Hilbert space H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
, an entangled state can be constructed as

\displaystyle|\psi_{E_{-}}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(|0\rangle\otimes|1\rangle+|1\rangle\otimes|0\rangle\right)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(|01\rangle+|10\rangle\right)

with the Dirac “bra-ket” notation, which can also be expressed equivalently by using the spin-like “column-row” notation as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{E_{-}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\binom{1}{0}\otimes\binom{0}{1}+\binom{0}{1}\otimes\binom{1}{0}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(259)

which is actually one of four Bell states. In general, Bell states are maximally entangled pure states in Hilbert space H
{}_{S}\otimes
H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
, but they cannot be separated into pure states of each H
S
and H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
.

Let us make a detailed discussion on the maximally entangled pure states in H
{}_{S}\otimes
H
{}_{S^{\prime}}
based on the real basic qubit-basis
\{\varsigma_{s}\}
defined in Eq.(
35
). Applying for
2-product qubit-basis states

\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}
defined in Eq.(
64
), we can express a biqubit-spinor state into the following general form:

\displaystyle\Psi=\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}}\,\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}\varsigma_{s_{1}s_{2}}\equiv\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}}\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}\,\varsigma_{s_{1}}\otimes\varsigma_{s_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{++}\\
\psi_{+-}\\
\psi_{-+}\\
\psi_{--}\end{pmatrix},

(260)

with
s_{1},s_{2}=\pm
. where
\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}
denote four constant amplitudes. The four Bell states as maximally entangled pure states in such a spinor representation can be read from the above biqubit-spinor state with the following conventions:

\displaystyle\psi_{E_{\pm}}=\hat{\gamma}_{\pm}\Psi,\quad\psi_{E^{\prime}_{\pm}}=\gamma_{5}\hat{\gamma}_{\pm}\Psi,\quad\psi_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\hat{\gamma}_{5}),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{5}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3},\quad\gamma_{5}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(261)

where
\psi_{E_{\pm}}
and
\psi_{E^{\prime}_{\pm}}
are regarded as chiral biqubit-spinor states.

To be more explicit, let us write down four Bell states in the spinor representation as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{E_{+}}=\psi_{++}\varsigma_{+}\otimes\varsigma_{+}+\psi_{--}\varsigma_{-}\otimes\varsigma_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\\
0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{E_{-}}=\psi_{-+}\varsigma_{-}\otimes\varsigma_{+}+\psi_{+-}\varsigma_{+}\otimes\varsigma_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{E^{\prime}_{+}}=\psi_{++}\varsigma_{+}\otimes\varsigma_{+}-\psi_{--}\varsigma_{-}\otimes\varsigma_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\\
0\\
-1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{E^{\prime}_{-}}=\psi_{-+}\varsigma_{-}\otimes\varsigma_{+}-\psi_{+-}\varsigma_{+}\otimes\varsigma_{-}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
-1\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

(262)

So that the general entangled states can be represented as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{E_{+}}=\hat{\gamma}_{+}\Psi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{++}+\psi_{--})\,\psi_{E_{+}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{++}-\psi_{--})\,\psi_{E^{\prime}_{+}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{++}\\
0\\
0\\
\psi_{--}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Psi_{E_{-}}=\hat{\gamma}_{-}\Psi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{+-}+\psi_{-+})\,\psi_{E_{-}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\psi_{+-}+\psi_{-+})\,\psi_{E^{\prime}_{-}}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\psi_{+-}\\
\psi_{-+}\\
0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle|\psi_{++}|^{2}+|\psi_{--}|^{2}=1,\quad|\psi_{+-}|^{2}+|\psi_{-+}|^{2}=1,

(263)

which indicates that a general entangled state for two qubits can be expressed as a superposition of Bell states and meanwhile constructed by a chiral qubit-spinor state with global amplitudes constrained by the total probability.

From the above analyses, we come to the statement that by adopting qubit basis states in the spinor representation, an entangled state in quantum physics can formally be expressed into a chiral qubit-spinor state. Therefore, a general entangled state in quantum physics can be regarded as an entangled state of qubits with the probability amplitudes satisfying the normalization condition constrained from the total probability.

It is noticed that all above analyses and discussions about entangled state as an inseparable state in the tensor product Hilbert space are actually applicable to local states of qubits rather than only global states. Namely, they should hold for both global and local coherent states of qubits.

Let us now turn to investigate local coherent state of qubits as qubit-spinor field. It is demonstrated in the previous section that a self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field becomes more appropriate to build the action, so that all
\Gamma
-matrices satisfy the anticommutative relations. Meanwhile, the group algebra of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries can simply be formed from the commutators of
\Gamma
-matrices. In analogous to the above analyses and discussions on entangled states in quantum physics as global coherent states of qubits, we come to a similar statement that a self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field is conceptually regarded as an
entangled qubit-spinor field
. For that, we are led to make the following postulate.

Locally entangled-qubits motion postulate
: a locally entangled state of qubits as a column vector field of local distribution amplitudes obeying the maximum coherence motion principle brings on an
entangled qubit-spinor field
which is postulated to be as
fundamental building block of nature
. Such an entangled qubit-spinor field can generally be expressed as a
self-conjugated chiral qubit-spinor field
.

VIII.2
Majorana-Weyl type fermions as entangled qubit-spinor fields in category-2
\And
category-3 and the inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetries associating with inhomogeneous Lorentz group symmetries in four- and six-dimensional Minkowski spacetimes

The actions in Eqs.(
110
) and (
117
) indicate that the self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
in category-3 defined in Eq.(
IV.7
) characterizes a massless Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
in six-dimensional spacetime. Whereas in such a triqubit-spinor representation, the eight-dimensional Hilbert space spanned by 3-product qubit basis states is not large enough to make all
\Gamma
-matrices anti-commuting. As shown in Eq.(
253
), it is more appropriate to extend eight-dimensional Hilbert space to 16-dimensional Hilbert space spanned by 4-product qubit basis states with a chiral structure so as to keep the same independent degrees of freedom.

Let us begin with introducing
entangled triqubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x)
, which are defined by the following self-conjugated chiral triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\pm}(x)
:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}-}(x)\equiv\Gamma_{-}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{L}(x)}{\psi_{L}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}+}(x)\equiv\Gamma_{+}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{R}(x)}{\psi_{R}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\hat{\gamma}_{7}),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{7}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5},

(264)

where
\psi_{L}
and
\psi_{R}
are the usual left-handed and right-handed Weyl fermions defined by the Dirac fermion
\psi_{D}(x)
as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{L,R}=\gamma_{\mp}\psi_{D},\quad\gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\gamma_{5}),

\displaystyle\psi_{L,R}^{\;\bar{c}}=C_{D}\bar{\psi}_{L,R}^{T},\quad C_{D}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

(265)

with
\psi_{L,R}^{\;\bar{c}}
being complex charge-conjugated ones. In general,
\psi_{D}
can be regarded as complex biqubit-spinor field
\psi_{D}\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{2}}
, and
\psi_{L,R}
are viewed as chiral complex biqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{L,R}\equiv\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{2}}
.

The self-conjugated triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)
can be expressed as the superposition of two entangled triqubit-spinor fields, i.e.:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)+\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)=\binom{\psi_{D}(x)}{\psi_{D}^{\bar{c}}(x)},

(266)

where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x)
satisfy the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}^{T}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x),\quad C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes C_{D},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{7}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x)=\pm\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{7}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}^{c}(x)=\pm\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}^{c}(x).

(267)

Such defined entangled triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x)
are also considered as
Majorana-Weyl type fermions
. To be distinguishable with ordinary
left-handed and right-handed Weyl fermions
defined in four-dimensional spacetime via the project operators
\gamma_{\mp}=\frac{1}{2}(1\mp\gamma_{5})\equiv\gamma_{L,R}
, the entangled qubit-spinor fields defined with the project operators
\Gamma_{\mp}=\frac{1}{2}(1\mp\hat{\gamma}_{D+1})
in high dimensional spacetime are referred to as
westward qubit-spinor field and eastward qubit-spinor field
, respectively. So that,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)
is called as
westward entangled triqubit-spinor field
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)
as
eastward entangled triqubit-spinor field
.

In terms of the westward and eastward entangled triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{3}}(x)
, the actions in Eqs.(
117
) and (
113
) in category-3 can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\frac{1}{2}[\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Gamma^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)]

(268)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)\delta_{k}^{\;m}\Gamma^{k}i\partial_{m}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)+\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)\delta_{k}^{\;m}\Gamma^{k}i\partial_{m}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)],

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{6}x\,\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)\delta_{\hat{a}}^{\;\hat{\mu}}\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}i\partial_{\hat{\mu}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x),

with
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
,
\,k,m=5,6
and
\hat{a},\hat{\mu}=0,1,2,3,5,6
labeling six-dimensional spacetime. We have introduced in the second equality the matrices
\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}
defined by
\Gamma
-matrices as follows:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\Gamma_{-},\quad\Gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{9}),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{9}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{7}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5}.

(269)

Where
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}
represents a westward entangled qubit-spinor field satisfying the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}=\binom{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}}{\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{L}(x)\\
\psi_{L}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)\\
\psi_{R}(x)\\
\psi_{R}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix}\equiv\Gamma_{-}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{4}},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}\bar{\psi}^{T}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{9}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}=-\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}},\quad\hat{\gamma}_{9}\psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}=-\psi^{c}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}},

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}=-i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}.

(270)

For convenience,
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}
is referred to as
entangled tetraqubit-spinor field
, which is also regarded as Majorana-Weyl type fermion.

In such a westward spinor representation, both
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\equiv(\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{k})
(
a=0,1,2,3
;
k=5,6
) and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-matrices
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
(
p=1,2,3
) can be expressed as the following forms:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{a}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma^{a},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{7}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{8}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{9}=i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{3},

(271)

with
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) defined in Eq.(
IV.7
). In terms of the entangeled tetraqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}
, it is easy to check that there exists no scalar coupling term in associating to
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
matrices. This is because the matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}
have different commutation relations with
\hat{\gamma}_{9}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle[\tilde{\Gamma}^{p},\hat{\gamma}_{9}]=0,\quad\{\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\hat{\gamma}_{9}\}=0.

(272)

It is noticed from the action in Eq.(
268
) that the correlation motion in the fifth and sixth spatial dimensions can occur only between two entangled triqubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)
, which indicates that each entangled triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{3}}(x)
(or
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{3}}(x)
) has a free motion only in four-dimensional spacetime. So that the action of entangled triqubit-spinor field
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)
can present an equivalent action for category-2 biqubit-spinor field in four-dimensional spacetime, i.e.:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\{\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)\delta_{a}^{\;\mu}\Sigma_{-}^{a}i\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)-\lambda_{2}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)\tilde{\Sigma}_{-}^{p}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)

(273)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{p}(x)\partial_{\nu}\phi^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{Q}(\phi_{p}(x)\phi^{p}(x))^{2}\},

with
a,\mu=0,1,2,3
and
p=1,2
. Where
\Sigma_{-}^{a}
and
\tilde{\Sigma}_{-}^{p}
matrices are given by the
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\hat{a}}
through the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{-}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{a}\Gamma_{-},\quad\tilde{\Sigma}_{-}^{p}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}\Gamma_{-},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{a}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma^{a},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\equiv\tilde{\Gamma}^{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{7}),

(274)

with
\gamma^{a}
(
a=0,1,2,3
) given in Eq.(
IV.7
).

In the entangled qubit-spinor representation, all
\Gamma
-matrices given in Eqs.(
VIII.2
) and (
VIII.2
) become anti-commuting,

\displaystyle\{\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\Gamma^{\hat{b}}\}=2\eta^{\hat{a}\hat{b}},\quad\{\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}\}=0,\quad\{\tilde{\Gamma}^{p},\tilde{\Gamma}^{q}\}=2\eta^{pq},

(275)

with
\hat{a},\hat{b}=0,1,2,3,5,6
,
p,q=1,2,3
, and

\displaystyle\{\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{b}\}=2\eta^{ab},\quad\{\Gamma^{a},\tilde{\Gamma}^{p}\}=0,\quad\{\tilde{\Gamma}^{p},\tilde{\Gamma}^{q}\}=2\eta^{pq},

(276)

with
a,b=0,1,2,3
,
p,q=1,2
.

The commutators of
\Gamma
-matrices provide directly the group generators of extended
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{\hat{a}},\Gamma^{\hat{b}}],\quad\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{\hat{a}}\Gamma_{-},\quad\Gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{9}),

(277)

which satisfy the following group algebra:

\displaystyle[\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}},\Sigma^{\hat{c}\hat{d}}]=i(\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{d}}\eta^{\hat{b}\hat{c}}-\Sigma^{\hat{b}\hat{d}}\eta^{\hat{a}\hat{c}}-\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{c}}\eta^{\hat{b}\hat{d}}+\Sigma^{\hat{b}\hat{c}}\eta^{\hat{a}\hat{d}}),

\displaystyle[\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}},\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{c}}]=i(\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}\eta^{\hat{b}\hat{c}}-\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{b}}\eta^{\hat{a}\hat{c}}),\quad[\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}},\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{b}}]=0.

(278)

A similar group algebra holds for
\Sigma^{ab}
and
\Sigma_{-}^{a}
,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{ab}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{b}],\quad\Sigma_{-}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{a}\Gamma_{-},\quad\Gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{7}).

(279)

The group algebra for the group generators
\Sigma^{ab}
or
\Sigma^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
characterize the motion-correlation
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) for
D_{h}=4
or
D_{h}=6
. The commutating group algebra for the group generators
\Sigma_{-}^{a}
or
\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}
brings on an Abelian-type group symmetry for the action of entangled qubit-spinor field.

Let us study explicitly such an Abelian-type group symmetry for both westward entangled triqubit-spinor field and westward entangled tetraqubit-spinor field. They transform under the group operation as follows:

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)=S(\varpi)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x),\quad S(\varpi)=e^{i\varpi_{a}\Sigma_{-}^{a}/2},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)\to\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)=S(\varpi)\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x),\quad S(\varpi)=e^{i\varpi_{\hat{a}}\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}/2},

(280)

with the explicit forms,

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(w)}(x)+\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(e)}(x),\quad S(\varpi)=1+i\varpi_{a}\Sigma_{-}^{a}/2,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(w)}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x),\quad\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(e)}(x)\equiv i\frac{1}{2}\varpi_{a}\Sigma_{-}^{a}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x),

(281)

and

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)=\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(w)}(x)+\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(e)}(x),\quad S(\varpi)=1+i\varpi_{\hat{a}}\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}/2,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(w)}(x)\equiv\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x),\quad\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(e)}(x)\equiv i\frac{1}{2}\varpi_{\hat{a}}\Sigma_{-}^{\hat{a}}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x),

(282)

where
\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(e)}(x)
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(e)}(x)
represent the shifted new parts of entangled qubit-spinor fields after such Abelian-type group transformations. The superscripts ‘
w
’ and ‘
e
’ on the entangled qubit-spinor fields label their westward and eastward chirality properties, respectively,

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{7}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(w)}(x)=-\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(w)}(x),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{7}\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(e)}(x)=+\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}^{(e)}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{9}\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(w)}(x)=-\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(w)}(x),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{9}\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(e)}(x)=+\tilde{\psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}^{(e)}(x).

(283)

Such an Abelian-type group symmetry reflects a translation-like spin group symmetry of entangled qubit-spinor field in Hilbert space. It is noted that the shifted new part of entangled qubit-spinor field gets a sign flip in chirality as shown in Eq.(
VIII.2
). Namely, when the entangled qubit-spinor field is westward, its shifted new part after the transformation becomes eastward, which holds the other way round.

This enlarged translation-like Abelian spin group symmetry is analogous to the translation group symmetry P
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
of coordinates in Minkowski spacetime. For convenience, we may use the notation W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
to denote such a translation-like Abelian spin group symmetry since it brings on a sign flip in chirality for the shifted new part after such a group transformation. As such a translation-like Abelian spin group symmetry W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
arises initially from the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate, it is regarded as an
entanglement-correlated Abelian-type spin group symmetry
in the entangled qubit-spinor representation of Hilbert space.

As such an entanglement-correlated translation-like spin group symmetry W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
is associated with the sign flip in chirality of westward and eastward entangled qubit-spinor fields for the initial and shifted new parts, we may refer to W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
as
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin symmetry
for short. The extended
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry is referred to as
inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry
which is denoted as follows:

\displaystyle WS(1,D_{h}-1)\equiv SP(1,D_{h}-1)\rtimes W^{1,D_{h}-1},

(284)

where the symbol ‘
\rtimes
’ is used to indicate that the inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) is a semidirect product group with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry group SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin symmetry group W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
.

The group generators of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry is simply given by

\displaystyle\Sigma^{pq}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{D_{h}+p},\Gamma^{D_{h}+q}]=\frac{i}{4}[\tilde{\Gamma}^{p},\tilde{\Gamma}^{q}]\equiv\tilde{\Sigma}^{pq},

(285)

with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=2
and
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=3
in correspondence to the entangled qubit-spinor fields
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}}(x)
and
\psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}}(x)
.

The actions in Eqs.(
273
) and (
268
) possess the following inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=WS(1,D_{h}-1)\times SP(q_{c})\equiv SP(1,D_{h}-1)\rtimes W^{1,D_{h}-1}\times SP(q_{c}),

(286)

with
D_{h}=4
and
D_{h}=6
for
q_{c}=2
and
q_{c}=3
, respectively. Such an inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry is in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry in Minkowski spacetime,

\displaystyle PO(1,D_{h}-1)=P^{1,D_{h}-1}\ltimes SO(1,D_{h}-1),

which is a semidirect product group symmetry with P
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
representing the translation group symmetry of coordinates in Minkowski spacetime.

In general, the maximal symmetry of the actions in Eqs.(
273
) and (
268
) can be expressed as the following associated symmetry with coincidental transformations:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,D_{h}-1}\ltimes SO(1,D_{h}-1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,D_{h}-1)\rtimes W^{1,D_{h}-1}\times SG(1)\times SP(q_{c})

(287)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,D_{h}-1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,WS(1,D_{h}-1)\times SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}),

with
D_{h}=4,6
for
q_{c}=2,3
, respectively. Where the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
” is used to indicate the associated symmetry in which the transformation of
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) in Hilbert space must be coincidental to that of isomorphic Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime. SP(
q_{c}
) represents the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry and SC(1)
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
SG(1) denotes the associated symmetry for global scaling transformations.

VIII.3
Entangled pentaqubit-spinor field as entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in category-0 and inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,9) as unified spin-color symmetry of chiral lepton-quark state in 10D hyper-spacetime

A massless Dirac fermion as triqubit-spinor field in category-3 is shown to be characterized by an entangled tetraqubit-spinor field with inhomogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry WS(1,5) in six-dimensional spacetime. Based on the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate, let us further examine an
entangled pentaqubit-spinor field
constructed from a chiral spinor structure of tetraqubit-spinor field in Category-0 with zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0
in ten-dimensional hyper-spacetime. Such an entangled pentaqubit-spinor field is verified to unify chiral spin-color lepton-quark states into an
entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
.

In light of chiral qubit-spinor structure of tetraqubit-spinor field, we are able to construct a
self-conjugated chiral pentaqubit-spinor field
as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)}{\Psi^{\,\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)},

(288)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)
is a
chiral complex tetraqubit-spinor filed
with
\Psi^{\;\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)
as the complex charge-conjugated one. They have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}++}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+-}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-+}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}--}^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{b}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{g}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{w}_{L}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Psi^{\,\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}++}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+-}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-+}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}--}^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{b\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{g\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)\\
Q^{w\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)\end{pmatrix},

(289)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\Psi^{\,\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x)=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}\bar{\Psi}^{\,T}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}(x),\quad C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{4}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{D},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}s_{1}s_{2}}^{2}}\equiv Q^{\alpha}_{L}(x)\equiv\gamma_{-}Q^{\alpha}(x),\quad\gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{5}),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}s_{1}s_{2}}^{2}}^{\bar{c}}\equiv Q^{\alpha\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)=C_{D}\bar{Q}^{\alpha\;T}_{L}(x),\quad C_{D}=-i\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle s_{1},s_{2}=\pm,\quad\alpha=\{s_{1}s_{2}\}\equiv(r,b,g,w),

(290)

where
Q^{\alpha}_{L}(x)
with
\alpha=r,b,g,w
represent the left-handed Dirac fermions with unified four color-spin charges in correspondence to the ‘red’, ‘blue’, ‘green’ and ‘white’. The first three color-spin charges ‘red’, ‘blue’ and ‘green’ characterize three chromo-quarks and the fourth color-spin charge ‘white’ reflects a relevant lepton.
Q^{\alpha}_{L}(x)
and
Q^{\alpha\,\bar{c}}_{L}(x)
are defined as left-handed lepton-quark state and its complex charge-conjugated one. Each
Q^{\alpha}_{L}(x)
is regarded as a
chiral complex biqubit-spinor field

\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}s_{1}s_{2}}^{2}}
.

Based on the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate, the self-conjugated chiral pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)
is referred to as
entangled pentaqubit-spinor field
, its action in ten-dimensional hyper-spacetime is built to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{10}x\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\Sigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x),

(291)

where
\Sigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}
(
\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
) matrices are defined with
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}=(\Gamma^{a},\Gamma^{A})
(
a=0,1,2,3,A=5,\cdots,10
), which have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}\Gamma_{-},\quad\Gamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{11}),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{11}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0}=\,\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{1}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}=i\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{6}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{7}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{8}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{9}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{10}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0},

(292)

where all
\Gamma
-matrices become anti-commuting,

\displaystyle\{\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}},\Gamma^{\mathsf{B}}\}=2\eta^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}.

(293)

In general,
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)
is also referred to as
entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
for chiral lepton-quark state, which satisfies the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}^{T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{11}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)=\hat{\gamma}_{11}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}^{c}(x)=-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{D},

(294)

It can be verified in the entangled pentaqubit-spinor representation that besides the
\Gamma
-matrices
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}
(
\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
) and
\hat{\gamma}_{11}
, there exists no any other anti-commuting
\Gamma
-matrix that can bring on scalar coupling term with the entangled pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)
. Therefore, the entangled pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)
has zero
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{Q_{c}}=q_{c}=0
and the action in Eq.(
291
) gets no scalar coupling.

The action in Eq.(
291
) possesses an associated symmetry in which the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,9) is in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,9) in ten-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime. Such an associated symmetry is presented as follows:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,9)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,WS(1,9)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}=0)

(295)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,9}\ltimes SO(1,9)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,9)\rtimes W^{1,9}\rtimes SG(1),

where we have used the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
” to indicate the associated symmetry which requires the transformation of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) be coincidental to that of Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,9)
\cong
SP(1,9). The group generators of WS(1,9) can directly be presented by the commutators of
\Gamma
-matrices:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}},\Gamma^{\mathsf{B}}],\quad\Sigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}\Gamma_{-},

(296)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
. Where
\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
represent the group generators of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) and
\Sigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}
the group generators of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin symmetry W
1,9
.

The hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) reflects a unified spin
\&
color charge symmetry for chiral lepton-quark state. To be more explicit, it is natural to decompose the hyperspin symmetry SP(1,9) into the following subgroups:

\displaystyle SP(1,9)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle SP(1,3)\times SP(6)\cong SO(1,3)\times SO(6)

(297)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SU_{C}(4),

with the corresponding group generators,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3)\in sp(1,3)\cong so(1,3)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=5,6,7,8,9,10)\in sp(6)\cong so(6)\cong su_{C}(4).

(298)

Where the subgroup symmetry SP(1,3)
\cong
SO(1,3) in connection with ordinary four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime represents the usual spin symmetry, and the subgroup symmetry SP(6)
\cong
SO(6)
\cong
SU
C
(4) in correlation with extra six spacial dimensions characterizes a unified four color-spin charge symmetry of chiral lepton-quark state.

VIII.4
Entangled hexaqubit-spinor field in Category-1 as entangled hyperqubit-spinor field and inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as unified spin-color symmetry of lepton-quark state in 11D hyper-spacetime

It is known that quarks in the strong interaction of SM behave as vector-like Dirac fermions, which indicates that the entangled pentaqubit-spinor field as self-conjugated chiral pentaqubit-spinor field is not an appropriate entangled hyperqubit-spinor field for characterizing quarks in SM. We should consider an entangled hyperqubit-spinor field that contains both left-handed and right-handed lepton-quark states.

Let us introduce an entangled hyperqubit-spinor field with the following hyperqubit-spinor structure:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{5}}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{5}}(x)},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{5}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{4}}(x)}{\Psi^{\;\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{4}}(x)},\quad\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{5}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{4}}(x)}{\Psi^{\;\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{4}}(x)},

(299)

where
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{5}}(x)
are entangled pentaqubit-spinor fields defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}\pm}^{5}}(x)\equiv\Gamma_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)}{\Psi^{\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\hat{\gamma}_{11}),\;\;\hat{\gamma}_{11}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5}

(300)

with
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{5}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}(x)
the entangled pentaqubit-spinor field as shown in Eq.(
288
).
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
is a self-conjugated pentaqubit-spinor field in Category-1 with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=1
, which is formed from the following
complex tetraqubit-spinor field
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}++}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+-}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-+}^{2}}\\
\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}--}^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r}(x)\\
Q^{b}(x)\\
Q^{g}(x)\\
Q^{w}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Psi^{\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}++}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}+-}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}-+}^{2}}\\
\psi^{\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}--}^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{b\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{g\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{w\,\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}s1s2}^{2}}\equiv Q^{\alpha}(x),\;\;s_{1},s_{2}=\pm,\;\;\alpha\equiv\{s_{1}s_{2}\}\equiv(r,b,g,w),

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}s1s2}^{2}}^{\bar{c}}\equiv Q^{\alpha\,\bar{c}}(x)=C_{D}\bar{Q}^{\alpha\,T}(x)\equiv\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}s1s2}^{2}}^{\ast},

(301)

where the complex tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)
is composed of
complex biqubit-spinor fields

\psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}s1s2}^{2}}
that are regarded as Dirac fermion states
Q^{\alpha}(x)
. Such Dirac fermion states
Q^{\alpha}(x)
labeled with four color-spin charges ‘red’, ‘blue’, ‘green’ and ‘white’ form a lepton-quark state.
Q^{\alpha\,\bar{c}}(x)
is defined as the complex charge conjugation state of lepton-quark state
Q^{\alpha}(x)
.

The complex tetraqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)
can generally be written as the sum of chiral complex tetraqubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{4}}(x)
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{4}}(x)+\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{4}}(x),

(302)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{4}}(x)
and their complex charge-conjugated ones
\Psi^{\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{4}}(x)
are given by,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{4}}(x)\equiv\varGamma^{5}_{\pm}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r}(x)\\
Q^{b}(x)\\
Q^{g}(x)\\
Q^{w}(x)\end{pmatrix}_{R,L},

\displaystyle\Psi^{\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}\pm}^{4}}(x)\equiv\varGamma^{5}_{\mp}\Psi^{\;\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{b\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{g\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
Q^{w\,\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix}_{L,R},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{5}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\tilde{\gamma}_{5}),\quad\tilde{\gamma}_{5}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

(303)

which are related to the left-handed and right-handed lepton-quark states
Q^{\alpha}_{L,R}(x)
.

The action of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{11}x\,\{\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)-\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)\},

(304)

where
\varSigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}
matrices are defined with
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\hat{\mathsf{A}}}=(\varGamma^{a},\varGamma^{A},\varGamma^{12})
(
\hat{\mathsf{A}}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,12
,
a=0,1,2,3,A=5,\cdots,11
). Their explicit forms are given by,

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathsf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}\varGamma_{-},\quad\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\varGamma}\varGamma_{-},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=\;\sigma_{0}\otimes\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}},\quad\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10,

\displaystyle\varGamma^{11}=\,i\sigma_{1}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{11},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{12}=-i\sigma_{2}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{11}\equiv\tilde{\varGamma},

\displaystyle\varGamma_{-}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\hat{\gamma}_{13}),\quad\hat{\gamma}_{13}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{11},

(305)

with
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}
(
\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
) defined in Eq.(
VIII.3
). The entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)
satisfies the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}^{T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{13}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)=\hat{\gamma}_{13}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}^{c}(x)=-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{D}.

(306)

We may refer to
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)
as
entangled hexaqubit-spinor field
.

The action in Eq.(
304
) possesses an associated symmetry in which the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,10) is in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,10). The whole associated symmetry of the action can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,10)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,WS(1,10)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(1)

(307)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle SC(1)\ltimes P^{1,10}\ltimes SO(1,10)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,10)\rtimes W^{1,10}\rtimes SG(1)\times O(1),

where the group transformations of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,10) and Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,10) must be coincidental as indicated by the symbol “
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
”. The group generators of WS(1,10) are simply presented by the commutators of anti-commuting
\varGamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}],\quad\varSigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}\varGamma_{-},

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,11
. The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(1)
\cong
O(1) becomes manifest with transformation
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)\to-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)
, which is coincidental to the chirality property of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
\hat{\gamma}_{13}\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)=-\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}(x)
.

The hyperspin symmetry SP(1,10) can be decomposed into the following subgroups:

\displaystyle SP(1,10)\supset SP(1,3)\times SP(7)\cong SO(1,3)\times SO(7)

\displaystyle\supset SO(1,3)\times SU_{C}(4)\supset SO(1,3)\times SU_{C}(3),

(308)

with the subgroup generators,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3)\in sp(1,3)\cong so(1,3)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=5,\cdots,10)\in sp(6)\cong so(6)\cong su_{C}(4).

(309)

The unitary symmetry SU
C
(4) is regarded as color-spin charge symmetry of lepton-quark state, which is correlated to extra six dimensions in Minkowski hyper-spacetime of coordinates. The subgroup symmetry SU
C
(3) of SU
C
(4) produces the strong interaction symmetry of three vector-like chromo-quarks in SM.

To show explicitly the vector-like property of color-spin symmetry SU
C
(3), it is useful to express the action in Eq.(
304
) into the following equivalent one by using the self-conjugated pentaqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{6}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{11}x\,\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\delta_{\mathsf{A}}^{\;\mathsf{M}}\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}i\partial_{\mathsf{M}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\phi(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\tilde{\Gamma}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\},

(310)

where the
\Gamma
-matrices are given by,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}=\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}},\;\;\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{11}=i\hat{\gamma}_{11},\quad\tilde{\Gamma}=I_{32},

(311)

with
\Gamma^{\mathsf{A}}
(
\mathsf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,10
) defined in Eq.(
VIII.3
) and
I_{32}
the
32\times 32
unit matrix. In obtaining the above action, we have used the following identity resulting from the definitions given in Eq.(
VIII.4
):

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}-}^{5}}(x)+\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}+}^{5}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}^{c}(x)=C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{5}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}^{T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x),

(312)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{5}}(x)
is defined in Eqs.(
VIII.4
) and (
VIII.4
) with lepton-quark state formed by Dirac fermions.

VIII.5
Entangled Heptaqubit-spinor field in Category-2 as entangled hyperqubit-spinor field and inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as unified spin-color-flavor symmetry of lepton-quark states in 12D hyper-spacetime

The lepton-quark state appearing in category-1 entangled hexaqubit-spinor field does characterize the vector-like strong interaction in SM, but it cannot reflect the flavor property of leptons and quarks in the weak interaction of SM, which brings us to consider the next category-2 entangled hyperqubit-spinor field with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=2
. Such an entangled hyperqubit-spinor field is referred to as
entangled heptaqubit-spinor field
with the following qubit-spinor structure:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{7}}(x)\equiv\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}^{\,c}(x)}\equiv\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L\,c}(x)},

(313)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}(x)
is regarded as a
chiral hexaqubit-spinor field
with
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}^{\,c}(x)
as its complex charge-conjugated one.
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}^{\,c}(x)
can be provided from a
self-conjugated hexaqubit-spinor field

\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x)
via the following definitions:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L}(x)\equiv\varGamma_{-}^{5}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}^{\,c}(x)=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}^{T}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L\,c}(x)=\varGamma_{+}^{5}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{\,c}(x)=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}(x),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{\pm}^{5}=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\tilde{\gamma}_{5}),\quad\tilde{\gamma}_{5}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{6}}=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}=\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{D}.

(314)

which have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\bar{c}}(x)\\
\Psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}^{\;\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix}_{L},\quad\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{S}}^{6}}^{L\;c}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\bar{c}}(x)\\
\Psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{15}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5}.

IX
Entangled octoqubit-spinor field as ultra-grand unified qubit-spinor field in 14D hyper-spacetime with inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as ultra-grand unified symmetry and the comprehension on lepton-quark state with one more family and the observed universe with 4D spacetime

Three entangled hyperqubit-spinor fields with respective to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charges
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}=q_{c}=0,1,2
and hyper-spacetime dimensions
D_{h}=10,11,12
have been analyzed to show how they characterize the basic properties of lepton-quark states, such as color-spin and flavor-spin charges. Nevertheless, their maximal inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry cannot yet produce the basic symmetries of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM though the entangled heptaqubit-spinor field contains
{\mathcal{D}}_{H}=64
independent degrees of freedom which is equal to the degrees of freedom involved in each family of leptons and quarks in SM. To realize the basic symmetries of leptons and quarks in SM as subgroup symmetries of hyperspin symmetry, it is natural to take into account, from the qubit-spinor categorization theorem, the next entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in category-3 based on the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate. Such an entangled hyperqubit-spinor field is referred to as entangled octoqubit-spinor field which spans 256-dimensional Hilbert space and has a free motion in 14-dimensional Minkowski hyper-spacetime. Its action will be shown to possess the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,13) which contains the subgroup symmetry SO(10) as grand unified group symmetry
GUT1
;
GUT2
, so that the basic symmetries of leptons and quarks in SM can be reproduced as subgroup symmetries of hyperspin symmetry SP(1,13). The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetry SP(3)
\cong
SU(2) is found to characterize the family-spin symmetry for two families of lepton-quark states in SM, which enables us to comprehend the basic issue on
why there exist more than one family of leptons and quarks in nature
. Meanwhile, we are able to provide a natural explanation on the longstanding open question
why our observed universe is only four-dimensional spacetime
.

IX.1
Entangled octoqubit-spinor field in 14D hyper-spacetime with inhomogeneous hyperspin and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin symmetries in producing basic symmetries of leptons and quarks in SM and comprehension on lepton-quark state beyond one family in nature

In the weak interaction of SM, either leptons or quarks as left-handed Weyl fermions always form weak isospin doublet. In the strong interaction of SM, quarks appear as vector-like Dirac fermions. In the electromagnetic interaction of SM, both charged leptons and quarks behave as vector-like Dirac fermions. In the entangled qubit-spinor representation, the hyperspin symmetry of entangled pentaqubit-spinor field in category-0 reflects a unified color-spin symmetry of chiral lepton-quark state, which is not suitable to characterize either the electromagnetic interaction of vector-like charged leptons and quarks in SM or the strong interaction of vector-like quarks in SM. Though the hyperspin symmetries for both entangled hexaqubit-spinor field in category-1 and entangled heptaqubit-spinor field in category-2 can describe the vector-like properties of charged leptons and quarks in the electromagnetic and/or strong interactions, but they cannot realize the weak isospin symmetry of chiral leptons and quarks in SM. Following along the qubit-spinor categorization theorem, we are going to present a detailed analysis and discussion on the entangled octoqubit-spinor field as hyperqubit-spinor field in category-3.

To characterize the chiral weak isospin property, let us begin with a category-3
chiral complex heptaqubit-spinor field

\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{7}}(x)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{7}}(x)\equiv\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}-}^{6}}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}+}^{6}}(x)}\equiv\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}^{L}(x)}{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}^{R}(x)},

. They have the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi^{u}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
\Psi^{d^{\prime}\,\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
\Psi^{d}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\
-\Psi^{u^{\prime}\,\bar{c}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\end{pmatrix},\quad\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{6}}^{R,L}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi^{u}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}}^{4}}(x)\\

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}=\,\sigma_{3}\otimes\varGamma^{A},\quad A=5,\cdots,14,

IX.2
Ultra-grand unified qubit-spinor field in 14D hyper-spacetime with inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as ultra-grand unified symmetry and comprehension on the observed universe with only 4-dimensional spacetime

The entangled octoqubit-spinor field is shown to be the minimal entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, which realizes all basic symmetries in SM as a direct product subgroup symmetry of hyperspin symmetry and obtains the action with a free motion in 14-dimensional hyper-spacetime. Nevertheless, our currently observed universe is only four-dimensional spacetime. How to understand such a universe with four-dimensional spacetime from 14-dimensional hyper-spacetime becomes an inevitable question to be answered in exploring the foundation of the hyperunified field theory. In fact, the question why our observed universe is only four-dimensional spacetime has been a longstanding and essential issue. Following along the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate and noticing the fact that our observed universe is composed of the atomic matter as basic constituent of observable matter world, we are able to provide a natural explanation on such a basic question.

From the above analyses, we come to the observation that if there exists only one family of chiral type leptons and quarks in nature, their maximally correlated motion brings on a free motion just in four-dimensional spacetime. Whereas the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate and maximum coherence motion principle inevitably lead to the prediction on the simultaneous existence of two families of chiral type leptons and quarks in nature with free motion in 14-dimensional hyper-spacetime. Let us suppose that all chiral type leptons and quarks become massive with different masses, and meanwhile the heavy leptons and quarks decay into light leptons and quarks. Such a situation does occur in the real case of SM, all heavy massive charged leptons and quarks decay eventually into the lightest massive electron and neutrinos as well as up quark and down quark. All the lightest leptons and quarks belong to a single family as the first family in SM. In fact, the observed matter in our living universe consists of the atomic matter which is just composed of the lightest up and down quarks together with electron. When combining such a fact with the observation that each twisting entangled hyperqubit-spinor field as grand unified qubit-spinor field (
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}^{-}}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}_{i}^{-}}^{7}}(x)
) contains exactly a single family of chiral type lepton-quark state and has a free motion only in four-dimensional spacetime, we arrive at the conclusion that the lightest up and down quarks together with electron can only keep a free motion in four-dimensional spacetime since they belong to a single family of lepton-quark state as the first family in SM and constitute the observed atomic matter in our universe. In other word, all existing matter in our living universe can only move in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, which brings about a natural explanation on the longstanding issue
why our observed universe is only four-dimensional spacetime
.

Hyperunified qubit-spinor field as fundamental building block of nature and inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry as hyperunified symmetry from maximum locally entangled-qubits motion principle

Based on the locally entangled-qubits motion postulate, four categories of entangled hyperqubit-spinor fields with respective to categoric

{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}

From the unification point of view, it appears a necessity to attribute such an intrinsic family-spin symmetry into a motion-correlation hyperspin symmetry so as to obtain a single hyperunified symmetry in hyper-spacetime, which comes to the main purpose in this section. For that, we are led to make the
least
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}

and propose the

as guiding principle, which enables us to construct a hyperunified qubit-spinor field of lepton-quark states as fundamental building block of nature.

\displaystyle\varGamma^{15}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tilde{\gamma}_{5},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{16}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\tilde{\gamma}_{5},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{17}=i\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{15},

\displaystyle\varGamma^{18}=i\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{15},

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{19}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{15}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{17},

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}_{5}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\gamma_{5},

\varGamma^{a}

It can be checked that

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\equiv C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{9}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}^{8}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{W}}^{7}},

To characterize explicitly the family-spin charge of lepton-quark states in SM, let us consider an alternative qubit-spinor structure of entangled enneaqubit-spinor field based on the twisting entangled hyperqubit-spinor field as grand unified qubit-spinor field

with
